# Camelot Systems News Archive

{{heading |level=2 |title=January 2003}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2003-01-24T01:25:13Z |title=Camelot Systems - R.I.P. 1998-2002 |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

R.I.P

{{img |file=Camsys.jpg |alt=Camelot Systems}}

1998-2002

In July 2002, Camelot Systems accepted its fate and unofficially announced its death, while leaving its existing website and resources online to be used by those still having a need for it.

However, due to the ingratitude and irrespect of many, it has been considered that Camelot Systems was no longer worth maintaining and being kept online. No, it shall not serve as a publicity stand for organizations that were founded by the very same people that bashed Camelot Systems and its staff with the utmost inconsideration of the time and efforts that were put just to serve them better, and the incomprehension that Camelot Systems had done its time, and that nothing else could be done to save it after the numerous attempts that were made to do so.

Nonetheless, I would like to address our sincere thanks to everyone who supported us through our ups and downs. I would also like to thank our friends that helped us during all these years, doing the best of what could be done for the community. Camelot Systems probably wouldn't have made it if it wasn't for our pleasure of providing game customization software and services for persons that enjoyed them even more than we had making them. There's probably too many of these persons to even bother making a list; and these persons already know who they are. Many thanks again, it's been a really fun time.

Many thanks as well to our formidable host, [ReconGamer](https://web.archive.org/web/20030124012513/http://www.recongamer.com/), who provided us great hosting services that allowed us to live longer, and not die abruptedly without explanations, two years ago.

As for the other ones I've mentioned above... You also know who you are. I hope you realize one day that there is real persons behind the names you see on the net, and being that inconsiderate of your sayings and actions actually does harm, just as in Real Life(tm).

Farewell...
- Veldhar Windrider, aka: Gold Dragon
Former leader of Camelot Systems
{{end-newsitem}}



{{heading |level=2 |title=July 2002}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2002-07-09T20:44:00Z |title=The Camelot castle awakens for WarCraft III |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys |summary=After the very successful release of WarCraft III by Blizzard last week, Camelot Systems is now on its way to provide third party tools to push even further the bounds of its customization. More details in the full story.}}

A month or two ago, Camelot Systems has deemed WarCraft III not to be a viable path to deliver customization tools for it as it was done for StarCraft, given the dramatically increased power of their map editor, WorldEdit.

Less than a week after its public release, WarCraft III is meeting the expectations, and more. The campaigns are well-thought and beautiful, multiplayer games have great gameplay, and the computer AI is invincible (as far as we know, no one has been able to beat it on 1v1 yet). The map editor is very good, albeit a bit awkward on its interface, but most importantly, the JASS script language (triggers) unveils an incredible power to any map maker who knows how to make proper use of it.

But even after all this, it is still possible to expect third party tools to deliver even more new features to WarCraft III. How so? Among things, custom textures and custom models are **not** supported by WorldEdit, which, even with the huge amount of features it provides, is still a great lack to customization.

For this reason, Camelot Systems is currently working hard on texture and model conversion tools to push WarCraft III customization bounds even further than originally intended. Further topics of customization possibilities are also being explored currently.

You can expect such tools to be released shortly, as a lot of progress has been made in the past few days. Here is a screenshot as for a sample of what it is possible to do so far with such tools:

[Now, where has the sheep gone?](http://www.camsys.org/windrider/war3/cubesheep.jpg)

In the meantime, have fun with WarCraft III! It is a game that was extremely worth the wait, both in terms of playing and customization. Stay tuned to Camelot Systems for further news on the tools development!
{{end-newsitem}}



{{heading |level=2 |title=June 2002}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2002-06-18T05:02:00Z |title=Temporary DNS downtime |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys |summary=Our camsys.org DNS is temporarily unavailable due to a required IP update. Please bookmark the address http://66.250.66.157/ until the issue is fixed.}}

It appears that our hosts, ReconGamer, have proceeded to a possible ISP change, which has also brought a complete change of IPs. As our DNS is still pointing to the old address, our website is currently unavailable through the normal http://www.camsys.org/ address.

Mutant of the ORG network is maintaining the DNS himself. As soon as we can contact him, everything should be back to normal. As Mutant is currently unavailable, if you know or have any contact to him, please ask him to check his e-mail at warcraft.org for further information.

During the meantime, please pass the word to those you know in the Camsys community.

Sorry for the inconvenience, and thanks for your cooperation.
{{end-newsitem}}



{{heading |level=2 |title=May 2002}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2002-05-26T01:32:00Z |title=MPQDraft for Windows v1.01 Maintenance Release |author=Quantam |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys |summary=MPQDraft for Windows v1.01 Maintenance Release}}

Well, I've been playing Warcraft III beta way too much lately (pretty much every free minute since I got out of school on Wednesday). But, I think I'm starting to go blind by playing it all day long. So I decided to do something nice for you guys, and work on MPQDraft.

There wasn't really a need for any major changes, but there were a few moderately large bugs that happen in very limited circumstances.

The full list of changes:
- Fixed "failed to distribute map" bug when playing SC Battle.net games
- Now works with War3 (I think; at least it doesn't give any error messages :-p)
- Now allows you to run MPQDraft on programs that don't use MPQs (so that you can use plugins)
- Made the patching error messages more descriptive

You can get it from the featured project link above.
{{end-newsitem}}



{{heading |level=2 |title=March 2002}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2002-03-17T06:05:00Z |title=Camsys fully operational, at last |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys |summary=The server problems we have experienced have finally been fixed, and at popular request, the website is now back operational despite the planned general brainstorming downtime brought with the arrival of WarCraft III.}}

There has been several problems into contacting our hosts, but once it has been done, everything went well and fast; the server is now fully operational without a single problem.

However, the website has not been put back online right after the fix. Why? The reason is simple: confusion still reigns as to the heading of Camelot Systems for the next months. With the arrival of WarCraft 3 Beta, the wave of excitation surrounding it gave one of the last final blows to StarCraft. But what about WarCraft 3?

Most of the Camsys community has expected Camelot to live back with the arrival of WarCraft 3; however, much has changed since all these years. The StarCraft modding community has been absolutely successful at proving Blizzard the power and influence that customization has on the lifetime of a great classic game. This move has been heard, and WarCraft 3 has been made surprisingly customizable; even with just its beta looks.

Nothing is known about WorldEdit yet, but according to the files specifications we have looked through, very little seems impossible for now.

* The scripting language is most likely the best thing that could have happened to any dreaming modder; as was promised, you can do about *anything* you want, when you want, in your maps. Forget everything about the StarCraft triggers system; there's now a **real** programming language coming with WarCraft III. No need to say things like custom campaigns, AI development or RPG maps making will be *aeons* ahead of whatever StarCraft had.

* Without any doubts, all units will be able to have new balance statistics and new names, as was allowed by StarEdit before. Additionally, many other statistics should be modifiable too; there is a dazingly number of fields found in the excel files used for units in WarCraft 3, like the base amount of gold/lumber a worker can carry, the capacity of certain units/buildings, or the circle radius the acolytes form around a gold mine while extracting its gold. All modifiable very easily through a single number or data value.

* The topic of new units is still rather complex to elaborate at this early point. It is almost sure every unit model will be skinnable through the use of texture images using a custom JPEG format. As for model themselves, **if and only if** Blizzard decides to support their customization, it will most likely be within the form of a 3ds max plugin. No need to say the sum of work required would probably be quite more heavy than what SC had, though.

* Custom campaigns should be much more supported by the game itself now, by allowing custom cinematics and ingame scenes, as well as automatic map cycle campaign handling, most likely handled by the scripting language itself. No need to say it should be much more easier to make complete campaigns featuring even non-linear plots by the time WarCraft 3 is released. No need to also say WarCraft 3 campaigns will most likely take advantage of this too.


So, with all these exciting new features found within WarCraft 3, most likely all benefitting to every modder out there, what about Camelot Systems? Unfortunately, this means that we'll have *very little, if anything at all* to do with WarCraft 3. The only point where we *might* have to intervene are models, for new models might not be supported directly by WorldEdit. Fortunately, it should be quite possible to make a 3rd party 3ds max plugin to export to WarCraft 3 models, as I have been able to decipher the model files quite well with minimal problems. However, for the rest, there shouldn't be much need for any important 3rd party tool like StarCraft had; perhaps small side tools, but nothing as important as StarDraft or StarGraft. Additionally, the WarCraft 3 Beta License Agreement prevents any such modification tool by the NDA clause, which means that no release or progress news can be made until the retail version at least hits the shop shelves (to be grabbed seconds later), which won't be happening for at least a few other months. In other words, **Camelot Systems doesn't have much future with WarCraft 3**.

The following question is quite obvious: **"So what now?"**. Camelot Systems has been created and has lived by providing many customization tools for the popular and classic games WarCraft II and StarCraft. Things have been changing over and over during the past year and a half, without any success. WarCraft III might not be the long-awaited solution. "So what now?" This is what our brainstorming is about. More to come later, most likely.

In the mean time, have fun, and enjoy the WarCraft III beta if you're one of the few and proud Beta Testers! If not, feel free to participate in our [forums](http://www.camsys.org/forums/) and chatroom located on [DAL.net](http://www.dal.net/) in #camsys. The downloads are also finally brought back online with the website comeback, so for everyone who has been waiting for them, don't wait any longer!

Until the next time...
{{end-newsitem}}



{{heading |level=2 |title=January 2002}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2002-01-27T04:31:00Z |title=Technical Support filled, Programmers still missing. |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys |summary=Following to the last week's staff openings announcement, we are pleased to see the high number of applications for Technical Support positions. However, many programmers are still missing to the call.}}

10 days have passed since the announcement, and many applications have been received so far. Thanks to everyone for your interest and support!

We have received far more applications for Technical Support than we had expected, which is a good sign of the continuing activity in the community to help fellow users in the usage of Camsys software and related. Additionally to the chosen staff filling Technical Support positions, honorary titles on the forums might be given to those who have proven to be of a great help to others over time as well! Thanks again for your high interest! The chosen technical support staff will be contacted within the next few days once the review is finalized.

However, on the programming field, things are quite the reverse. Very few applications have been received, and even less are actually 'new' potential staff. They will be reviewed shortly, along with the numerous Technical Support applications. While there should still be enough to maintain a few projects to be announced shortly, we still have much room available for new game related projects.

To this effect, here's a recall and some additions as of what we're looking for as programmers:

## Game Debugger
The x86 ASM language has no secret for you, and staring at whole assembly code pages to decode them is part of your numerous skills. Disassembly or SoftICE experience is a must. Knowledge of C/C++ is a good advantage, but not necessary.

## Game Programmer
You are highly talented in the programming field and have an excellent knowledge of C/C++ or a similar language. Possessing skills in gaming APIs like DirectX, OpenGL or equivalents is a must. You are very motivated in your projects. Knowing how to use the x86 ASM language efficiently is facultative, but a considerable bonus.

## Game Editor Programmer
You are experienced and comfortable using an High Level Language such as C++ Builder, Delphi, and/or Visual C++. You can easily create good user interfaces to allow the edition of existing game data. The possession of file deciphering skills is of a big help. Win32 and VCL usage is majorly preferred over MFC.

***Note to programmers:* Having your own project ongoing is a MAJOR bonus, as our current project pool is limited. Additionally, you must be able to provide some example of your past works as an e-mail attachment, or accessible URL.**

It is also to be noted that all positions require a fairly good amount of free time. After all, you're working here! :) Please also note this must not be seen as an obligation, but as a generic guideline of activity. In other words, it's nothing strict, it is just to make sure you don't actually 'die' (virtually) over time.

The benefits of working at Camsys are:

- Your project is seen by an audience and community of thousands of experienced gamers
- You have unlimited webspace, unlimited bandwidth and full SSS capacities (PHP, ASP, Perl, etc.) for your project's website, as accorded by our host, [ReconGamer](http://www.recongamer.com/). You only need to display an ad banner and the Camsys header on each of your pages, and there's no further limit to your creativity anymore!
- Staff is granted Auto-Op privileges, upon request, at our public chatroom #camsys on [DAL.net](http://www.dal.net/). Additionally, most of our programmers are often gathering there, meaning there's chances you can get direct help from fellow staff programmers on a trouble you're experiencing with your project.
- If anything's missing, you can suggest it directly and we might be able to accomplish your request, depending on its nature. Just try! :)


If one of these positions interest you and you think you can meet the exigences, then we are waiting for *you*! Send an e-mail to [hirings@camsys.org](hirings@camsys.org), and include the following informations:

- Real name, age
- Thorough description of your skills
- Past works example, as e-mail attachment or URL
- Why do you want to get in Camsys?
- What do you intend to be doing?

Due to the potentially high demand and number of applications, it might not be possible to reply to all of them; the selected persons will be contacted back by e-mail with a positive reply. Please accord us 1-2 weeks to review your submission.

For any questions, comments, suggestions, please adress them to {{highlight |text=Veldhar[GD]}} by e-mail at [GoldDragon@camsys.org](GoldDragon@camsys.org). You can also contact him through IRC by visiting #camsys on [DAL.net](http://www.dal.net/), in the evening EST times (GMT-5).

Thanks in advance for your participation and interest!
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2002-01-16T10:14:00Z |title=Camelot Staff Openings! |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys |summary=After a successful relaunch two weeks after its new opening, and a few management meetings to organize its new structure, Camelot Systems is now looking for new staff to pursue the new dynasty that has started!}}

It took time, but it's finally done. After fixing most of the major bugs that were found in the website and reorganizing Camelot, the Camelot Castle is now ready to be populated by a full complement of staffers!

Were you part of the previous Camelot dynasty and are motivated to get back in the crew to produce good work? Are you an experienced programmer or technical support expert and wish to get in on the action of a reknowned game customization and creation community? Is the taste of challenge and fan mail reward enough for your professional work? Then don't hesitate, and apply now!

The available staff positions are:

## Game Debugger
The x86 ASM language has no secret for you, and staring at whole assembly code pages to decode them is part of your numerous skills. Disassembly or SoftICE experience is a must. Knowledge of C/C++ is a good advantage, but not necessary.

## Game Programmer
You are highly talented in the programming field and have an excellent knowledge of C/C++ or a similar language. Possessing skills in gaming APIs like DirectX, OpenGL or equivalents is a must. You are very motivated in your projects. Knowing how to use the x86 ASM language efficiently is facultative, but a considerable bonus.

## Game Editor Programmer
You are experienced and comfortable using an High Level Language such as C++ Builder, Delphi, and/or Visual C++. You can easily create good user interfaces to allow the edition of existing game data. The possession of file deciphering skills is of a big help. Win32 and VCL usage is majorly preferred over MFC.

***Note to programmers:* Having your own project ongoing is a MAJOR bonus, as our current project pool is limited. Additionally, you must be able to provide some example of your past works as an e-mail attachment, or accessible URL.**

## Technical Support Expert
You really like to help others, and answering to technical support questions is part of your pastime, and the users appreciation, your reward. You know fairly well how to use the gaming software Camelot Systems is providing, and can help others to use them. Being able to write tutorials and help files is a big bonus.

It is also to be noted that all positions require a fairly good amount of free time. After all, you're working here! :)

If one of these positions interests you and think you can meet the exigences, then we are waiting for *you*! Send an e-mail to [hirings@camsys.org](mailto:hirings@camsys.org), and include the following informations:

- Real name, age
- Thorough description of your skills
- Past works example, as e-mail attachment or URL (N/A for Tech Support Experts)
- Why do you want to get in Camsys?
- What do you intend to be doing?

Due to the potentially high demand and number of applications, it might not be possible to reply to all of them; the selected persons will be contacted back by e-mail with a positive reply. Please accord us 1-2 weeks to review your submission.

Thanks in advance for your participation and interest!
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2002-01-02T09:14:00Z |title=Another year, another revolutionary program |author=Quantam |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys |summary=Camelot Systems does it again. Meet MPQDraft, the successor (and hopefully violent murderer) of StarDraft, once again revolutionizing the customization industry.}}

Haaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaappy New Year! Well, it's January 1st, so it must be that time again. That's right, it's time for me to release another program! And do I ever have a good one for you this time. I proudly present to you MPQDraft, the biggest step forward in mod making since MoPaQ 2000.

For those of you who have not heard about MPQDraft while it was in development and later in beta, it is a patch loader similar to StarDraft, even able to create self-executing patches. However, MPQDraft is far more than StarDraft ever was, and will hopefully be the fatal blow to StarDraft once and for all (as a Win2K user I do not like StarDraft). The major advantages of MPQDraft over StarDraft:
- Works on any version of Windows
- Works on any version of Starcraft, as well as any version of Diablo, Diablo 2 Warcraft 2 BNE, and any Blizzard program that uses MPQs (and I'm hoping it will also work on Warcraft 3)
- Has NO slowdown, as StarDraft used to
- Has NO files which it cannot patch (like StarDraft couldn't patch music files)
- Has no known bugs at this time (after being beta-tested for a while)
- But perhaps the biggest advantage is that MPQDraft supports patching plugins (i.e. StarGraft will be a plugin soon)

However, unlike StarDraft, MPQDraft does not include a patch creator. MPQDraft runs MPQ patches created by [MPQ2K](http://www.campaigncreations.com/starcraft/mpq2k/). In addition, MPQDraft will also run Diablo 2 Mod Manager mods (D2M files) just as well. But, unlike a normal mod created with MPQ2K, you do not need to replace patch_rt.mpq or ANY existing MPQ. You can even run multiple patches at once.

Anyways, enough babbling. Click here to download [MPQDraft](http://www.camsys.org/download.php?id=12) (Yes I know it doesn't have a manual yet. I'll get to it)
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2002-01-01T05:30:00Z |title=Welcome to the new Castle of Camelot! |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys |summary=After long months of reconstruction, managing, planning and development, Camelot Systems is now ready to unveil its new website and philosophy!}}

It took much time and efforts to bring Camelot Systems back online, but they have not been in vain. Welcome to the new Castle of Camelot!

Many changes have been done, and little is the same as before, except our name and devotion which are all the same: a complete dedication to the gaming world.

For many of you who have been visiting Camelot Systems during the past months, or even years, it has been known as being the reference for StarCraft customization software, and somewhat less for Descent 3, and a few other games. However, the new Camelot Systems is taking a new turn, as StarCraft has been declining for a long time due to its age, and the near arrival of WarCraft 3. This reason has led to the complete reconstruction of Camelot Systems, which is now restarting from scratch: website, and staff.

Camelot Systems will now be openly supporting most kinds of game-related projects: while keeping an important place to game customization software as we did for the past years, another important one will be made for serious game projects development done by the whole gaming community.

We will be running a massive hiring period during the following weeks, intended for all developers and/or leaders having *serious* customization software or game projects. It is to be noted that these projects should not be commercial intended, and should **not** be taken too lightly either; we do not want one-week-until-failure projects. While this will not be an obligation to finish your project, it is done to ensure we are only maintaining and promoting real, promising projects. More information on hirings will be given in the next few days. **Please do not start sending your applications now, or they will be ignored!**

As for our website, many features are working, and others are left for future soon development. Available are our software archives, survey(please vote!), account registration, news comments, and last but not least, our revamped forums, which are now directly part of the site's codebase. Feel free to take a look around and participate in the new forums. Shall you find bugs, please [e-mail me](mailto:GoldDragon@camsys.org?subject=Bug Report) with a thorough description of what you did and what occured, so I may fix them as soon as possible.

Happy new year to all our visitors, and may the new dynasty of Camelot start on this first day of 2002!
{{end-newsitem}}



{{heading |level=2 |title=December 2001}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-12-01T03:50:00Z |title=Progress Report #1 |author=Otak |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

It's been over a month since I last told you my progress on L2Edit, and it's about time I posted an update for you. In my last post, I think I was somewhere around deciphering the palettes; I've completed that, and have deciphered somewhere around 170 tiles, and have them in *.bmp form. Here's a pic of some random tiles:

{{img |file=tile_ie.gif |title=Random tiles}}

170 tiles might sound a lot to some of you, but there are countless more to do, I'm averaging about 30-40 a day but I don't have time to do this everyday. For the last week or so my schedule has been something like: School(8:10-2:45), Track(3:15-5:15), Nap(~5:30-9:00), Homework, Sleep. So I'm really looking forward to this weekend, and maybe I'll get more work done, but as for tonight it's not looking too good.

{{highlight |text=Veldhar[GD]}}, as some of you know, is working on a new layout for the site. I'm not supposed to give any details, but come to the [chatroom](/web/20011202192439/http://www.camsys.org/chat/chatroom.shtml) around 9:30-11:30PM EST and maybe {{highlight |text=Veldhar[GD]}} will tell you some more than I can.

Until the next update..
{{end-newsitem}}

{{heading |level=2 |title=October 2001}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-10-20T03:58:00Z |title=L2 Edit - One of many new projects |author=Otak |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

During the last 2 weeks or so, I have been working on a new project both for editing and practicing purposes for the upcoming Warcraft III: L2Edit. Its current function is to edit or create maps for the game *Lords of the Realm 2* by Sierra. So far, not an incredable amount of progress has been done; I've only recently finished my work on the palettes, which I would like to thank {{highlight |text=Ace Calhoon}} for the great amount of help.

Lords2 is an old, but still popular medieval Role Time Stratagy for DOS, and Windows. For more details, visit [Sierra's Website](https://web.archive.org/web/20020816042827/http://www.sierra.com/). Maps created with L2Edit should be able to work on multiplayer, but chances are the players will need to download the edited files/maps before being able to play. More information on this will show up as I progress. To view an example screen shot of the gameplay, I've uploaded a [screen shot](https://web.archive.org/web/20020816042827/http://www.camsys.org/Otak/L2Edit/screen.gif), using the palettes.

As for emailing me, since the site's email is still down, my other email address is [Wizard_Otak@yahoo.com](mailto:Wizard_Otak@yahoo.com).

I'll keep you updated.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-10-11T16:08:00Z |title=It's time I say 'hi!' |author=Sir Galahad |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Well, well. It's been over 3.5 years now since I first joined Camelot Systems, so I guess it's time for me to make a post and say hi. Hi world! Those of you who visit the forum have probably seen me posting there occasionally and a few others might have spotted me in the #CamSys channel on Dal.net (Which is still pretty empty, considering the amount of visitors on our forum and the counter on the downloads... Don't be shy, just hop in). But I can hear the rest think "Who are you?". Well, here goes:

In the early days of Camelot Systems, when StarCraft wasn't around yet (and seen by most of us as just a silly game of WarCraft in space), and the core of the editing community was still hanging around on the WarDraft forum, {{highlight |text=King Arthur}} attracted me, among others as a Support Technician for Camelot Systems, to give solutions to the problems people would have and post on the forums or email us directly. In addition to that, I would do a bit of data decrypting (sounds clever, but it just means finding out what the data in certain files actually meant) and a bit of programming. A few utilities for WarCraft II came from my hand, but troubles with my internet connection prevented me from carrying out my Tech Support part out to the fullest extend until much later. Although Camelot has evolved a bit from time to time, I just sticked around somewhere in a corner and am still a proud member.

Well, that's enough rambling about the past for one post, now on to the current times. As {{highlight |text=Defenderz}} has posted recently, the @camsys.org emails currently do not work correctly. Since this tend to take quite a while, it might be usefull for those with problems out there to know the alternative email addresses of the Support Technicians (Namely {{highlight |text=Ace Calhoon}} and myself) until the @camsys.org email addresses get back to work. You can email me at thels@wanadoo.nl and {{highlight |text=Ace Calhoon}} at ace_calhoon@hotmail.com. You can also use ICQ at 20800959 (me) or 9329218 ({{highlight |text=Ace Calhoon}}). Lastly, you might want to know what I'm up to nowadays. Well, it's called StarXed, named after the first unofficial map editor for WarCraft 2, namely War2Xed. StarXed will hopefully one day be an all around editor for StarCraft Scenario's, that should become an alternative to using StarEdit in combination with Emerald Patches. It's going to take quite a while before things get there though. I'll keep you informed.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-10-05T20:57:00Z |title=Good News and Bad News |author=Quantam |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Okay, first the good news. I finally got the last 2 known crashes fixed on MPQDraft. It took me so long because only 2 of the 50 people using MPQDraft (and I was not one of the 2) ever saw these crashes, so I didn't have much to go on. But, MPQDraft is feature-complete (actually it was feature complete in the last beta), and I'm about to release another beta to my testers. If no more serious bugs are found, it should be released publicly in a couple weeks.

Now the bad news, regarding the MP3/MIDI plugin for MPQDraft (officially named ThunderGraft). I was working on that project with {{highlight |text=Dark Brood}} from SC3D. In fact, he was doing about 2/3 of the work for it. But, he just suffered a hard drive crash, and lost all his work on it, in addition to some important school stuff. This does not mean the project is cancelled, it just means that it will be greatly delayed, as he has to rewrite it from scratch. But first he has to work manically to remake the school stuff he lost before it's due. I'll keep you posted on our progress.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-10-02T05:09:00Z |title=Updates... |author=Defenderz |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I would like to apologize for the downtime.  Recon moved servers causing some downtime, and this was compounded with an incorrect IP linked to the DNS, causing the experienced downtime.  We will try out best to never let this happen again.

As a small anecdote, {{highlight |text=Gold Dragon}} has written a five and a half page post detailing everything about himself and Camelot Systems, and as he puts it, “it got deleted by such a useful(sarcasm) feature of Internet Explorer which deletes a whole form with ESC.” So, he will post more in-depth shortly.  He is at college right now, and as with all of us in this school season, he is boggled down with work, causing all of his free time to be devoted to his work on improving portions of Camsys, thus minimizing the opportunities to make public appearances.  In short, he is hard at work and still quite alive.

As I had promised, I have given out a great bulk of information about HellGraft.  This can be found in the Round Table under the thread [HellGraft Q/A](https://web.archive.org/web/20020816042827/http://cgi.camsys.org/cbbs/UltraBoard.pl?Action=ShowPost&Board=roundtable&Post=273&Idle=30&Sort=0&Order=Descend&Page=0).  I encourage anyone with a question to go ahead and ask, I will answer as best as possible.

On that subject, my Camsys e-mail remains down, so I would appreciate people to continuing sending e-mail to [CoolGuyz00@yahoo.com](mailto:CoolGuyz00@yahoo.com) for me.  If you have e-mailed me at this address, and I haven’t responded yet, I have not forgotten about you, I haven’t had too much time yet to respond to e-mails, but I shall answer each and everyone within the next few days.

As a final note, I just want to say that all Americans right now (and forever) stand united (among ourselves and with most of the world).  All terrorism shall be brought to an end.

{{img |file=/web/20020816042827im_/http://www.camsys.org/news/dz/flag.jpg |title=US flag}}

GOD BLESS AMERICA
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-09-19T08:02:00Z |title=More Info on MPQDraft |author=Quantam |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

A number of people have been asking me about MPQDraft on the CamSys and Campaign Creations forums, as well as on ICQ. So I'll answer all those here.

But first of all, I should say that MPQDraft is DELAYED. Because, simply, I don't have the time. There is one major bug in it that I have just become aware of from two of my beta testers (which does not appear on Windows 2000, so I didn't spot it myself). I don't consider this a problem because I fully expect that I can find and fix it before release. But I have NO free time right now. In fact I won't be doing ANYTHING but school and homework until Friday afternoon (well, I might come online while I eat breakfast and dinner...).

- MPQDraft will not feature an MPQ editor, graphical or otherwise. I left this functionality up to MPQ2K and MoPaQ 2001, a GUI sibling of MPQ2K which is in the works. However, this is fourth on my programming to-do list (including MPQDraft).

- MPQDraft has the name "MPQ" in its name because MPQs will be the medium by which patches will be delivered. I have examined the alternatives, and using MPQs as patches FAR surpasses the capabilities of the alternatives. As for loading them, you can either load the MPQs manually, using MPQDraft (which requires you to manually select plugins), or...

- MPQDraft has the capacity to create SEMPQs. SEMPQs are functionally equivalent to SECWADs, except for all of the advantages MPQDraft offers over StarDraft (this includes the ability to use plugins). But, regardless of the plugins used, it will all be packed into a single EXE file.

- You will not be able to give custom icons to SEMPQs. When I first came up with the idea for MPQDraft (about a week before I started programming it), I intended for you to be able to use a custom icon in SEMPQs. But when I got to writing that particular code, I was shocked to find out that there is no real way to set an icon in an EXE after the EXE has been compiled from the source code (I'm sure {{highlight |text=BahamutZero}} is laughing at me right now, since Macs CAN do this). So, I regret to announce that this feature was dropped.

- MPQDraft plugins are executed at patch run-time, and add patching functionality. That excludes some things like an MPQ editor or ArsenalZero. StarGraft and WarGraft (as somebody proposed) loaders would be excellent examples of MPQDraft plugins (and as mentioned earlier, StarGraft is on my to-do list). However, you would need a separate program for creating patches to be loaded by plugins, which means that the old StarGraft would be used for creating PATs. And, as for {{highlight |text=Fusioned}}'s StarEx, I contacted him like four or five months ago about him making it an MPQDraft plugin, and he liked that idea. However, as with StarGraft, StarEx was broken by 1.08, and won't work until {{highlight |text=Fusioned}} creates all-new data-files for it.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-09-12T03:41:00Z |title=The Unspeakable Horror of 9-11 |author=Defenderz |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

This day shall forever live in infamy.  Tuesday, September 11, 2001, at 8:45 a.m. EST, one of the World Trade Center buildings was struck by a hijacked plane.  Not soon after, another renegade plane struck the other of the twin World Trade Center buildings.  While withstanding the actual crash, the heat from the fire made the steel supporting one of the buildings reach melting point at around 1,500º F., causing the structure to literally collapse upon itself, killing thousands people and emergency workers.  At 10:28 p.m. EST, the other of the struck buildings collapsed to the ground, again leaving up to possibly 10,000 or more people unaccounted for.  These events were accompanied by two more hijackings.  One of which struck the Pentagon, the center of military intelligence in the United States.  The fourth hijacked plane crashed southeast of Pittsburg.  It is speculated that this plane may have been attempting to reach Camp David.

Due to the aforementioned tragedies, all domestic U.S. flights were grounded, all international flights were routed to Canada, and the Canada and Mexican borders were closed.  Many businesses had to close today, and many schools had to be evacuated.  The New York Stock Exchange had never opened today, due to it’s proximity; however world markets have been seeing falls of 6% or more.

The attacks of today are speculated to be conducted by the international terrorist Osama bin Laden.  The intelligence of the United States is currently trying to find out who is behind this great tragedy, so we may take action against such person and any country harboring such person.  As for as casualties on American soil, the events of today are as immense as any has seen since the Civil War, and this day has been rumored to be the next "Pearl Harbor".

My heart and hope goes out to the people who had relatives working in the World Trade Center at the time of the bombing.  Searches have just commenced recently, which were halted early due to the impending collapse of the World Trade Building 7.  The events of today shall live on forever for us all, as a country, as a world.  I would like us all to pray for the people who have died in the horrific events of today.

Thank you for your time and respect.  God bless us all.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-09-09T07:23:00Z |title=Fear not; CamSys isn't dead |author=Quantam |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Far from it, actually. Since everybody is getting edgy, I thought I'd better explain what's going on. There are in fact at least 4 projects being worked on right now. I'll list them in order of their expected releases.

First of all, I am proud to announce that StarDraft will soon be very dead and gone. Why? Because it's successor is here: MPQDraft. MPQDraft, written by myself, works like StarDraft, patching temporarily, in memory. But it is much more than StarDraft ever was. First of all, MPQDraft is not limited to Starcraft; it will work with ANY Blizzard game that uses MPQs. Second, there are NO files that MPQDraft cannot patch (remember how StarDraft would crash on music files?). But the real clincher is that MPQDraft has a flexible, POWERFUL plugin system. Just how powerful is this plugin system? Read on; all of the other projects in development are MPQDraft plugins.

The second project is an MPQDraft plugin. It's called HellGraft, and is being developed by {{highlight |text=Defenderz}}. It allows you create custom spells and attacks in Diablo II, just like StarGraft did with Starcraft.

Next is an MPQDraft plugin that we just started on (myself and {{highlight |text=Dark Brood}} from Starcraft 3D). I can remember a good number of people on the CamSys and Campaign Creations forums wanting the ability to play MIDIs in Starcraft. Well, that's not quite what we're doing. No; this plugin will allow you to play MIDI OR MP3 music files in ANY Blizzard game. Does that sound like music to your ears?

But perhaps the thing which will excite you the most is this last one here. Before {{highlight |text=KramerBoy}} left, myself, {{highlight |text=KramerBoy}}, and {{highlight |text=Ius}} were working on updating StarGraft to 1.08. Well, now that {{highlight |text=KramerBoy}}'s gone, it's just {{highlight |text=Ius}} and me now. But we're still here, more or less. And someday, StarGraft will be coming back. I'm redoing the entire patching kernel, so it should be smaller and faster. But whatever happens, it will work with SC 1.08, AND it will work with Win2K, NT, XP, and all varieties of Windows. And, not surprisingly, it will also be an MPQDraft plugin.
{{end-newsitem}}



{{heading |level=2 |title=August 2001}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-08-28T10:38:00Z |title=Final Farewell |author=KramerBoy |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

This news post has been quite a long time coming, I know, and those of you who have paid any attention to the sorry state of this website and my involvement in it over the past couple of years have probably been expecting this for a while.

Two years, seven months, and twenty-four days ago, I joined a flourishing Camelot Systems with as little skill as I had potential.  Today, I find myself in much the same state.  In nearly three years I managed to finish only two projects:  SDTutor, a clumsy tutorial for a clumsy StarDraft, and ICE, a competent but needlessly complex editor for StarCraft's image scripts.  Only one of those two remains available here at CamSys.  In the gaping void between those two projects I managed to create nearly 100MB of programs and source code that never made it Camelot's surface; many you knew of, and many you didn't.  As a last gesture of goodwill to those who have been loyal to our cause over the years, a list and description of all the projects I've undertaken that died in progress is available on the forums.

Today, Tuesday August 28, 2001, is the last day I will spend as a Camelot Systems staff member.  Effective tomorrow I will no longer have anything to do with this website.  I will stop reading the email, I will terminate the ICQ account, and I will never again post on this website or its forums.  Except for the few close friends I have picked up along the way, none of you will ever hear from {{highlight |text=KramerBoy}} again.  If you have something to say, say it now.

My reason for leaving is cold, but simple:  I believe this website died when Andy Bond ({{highlight |text=King Arthur}}) left it.  He was the creative drive and the talent behind what was Camelot Systems; the rest of us were just the support staff.  When he left the website in my hands two summers ago, I was simply too inept to keep it afloat.  Hosting problems that should have been resolved in a matter of weeks instead took months.

Our prolonged downtime coupled with a decline in our software's popularity drove away most of our dedicated fanbase.  Case in point:  We used to serve nearly 2,500 downloads a day - yesterday we served less than a quarter of that number.  Since we resurfaced four months ago, no new projects have been completed, and only two programs have undergone any updates.  In the entire month of August, only one news post has made it to our front page, and with the school season just beginning, it's only going to get worse.

To Andy and Cameron, thank you for looking past my sheer lack of skill and for taking the time to instruct me.  Thank you for giving me such a great opportunity to expand my skills, and last but not least, thank you for that tour of Blizzard =).

To the rest of the Camelot Systems staff, I wish you all the best of luck and pray you can find a way to return this website to its former glory.

And to everyone who ever cared about Camelot Systems, I apologize for so mindlessly running it into the ground.

KB out.

*I'm passing all the days
through a window pane
And as the scene would change
I'd stay the same
This paradise was nothing new
But the paradise gets tired of you
    --Finger Eleven*
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-08-11T04:47:00Z |title=Forward in the past (antithesis to Back to the Future) |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

*(Note: This post was to be displayed 2 days ago; however, due to recent server troubles caused by the move completion, the news script was down. Sorry for the inconvenience.)*

Unless you didn't visit Camelot Systems for the past week, you've probably noticed many things have disappeared, like news posts, forum posts, new programs and such. Well, the server move completed, we were warned that there would be a loss of data between the backup time and the move time, so this is the result. However, I expected it to be a difference of a day or two instead of a full week. Oh well...

To summarize the news posts that have been deleted, {{highlight |text=Otak}} had added a new feature to the website, which is {{highlight |text=Replay of the Week}}. As its name suggests, it will feature a new replay each week that will surely be worth watching... or not ;) And as well, he released a new program for a game called {{highlight |text=Genders Wars}}, and obviously enough, it is named {{highlight |text=GenderDraft}}. It was available not so long ago, but the software had been deleted with the server move. {{highlight |text=Otak}} should re-upload it shortly.

I had also posted a news post (yeah, a page long or so), and since I don't have a backup of it, I can't repost it. So I'll try to summarize: I've been doing a giant catch-up on everything before leaving on a few days trip(which I'm back from). My lack of activity on the news was compensated by an intense work on two new projects, one that is quite big and game-related, the other is smaller but still hefty nonetheless, and is application-related. I'm not giving any other informations on these at the moment, but should be able to in a few weeks. The {{highlight |text=AI Tournament}} is still delayed due to myself being too busy with a little bit of everything, but it shouldn't be long before I post the official informations about it. Last of all, I've proceeded to create all remaining accounts for staff from the request e-mails I've received lately, so this matter is solved once and for all. As for the hosting requests, now that the server move is completed, I will be able to finalize the process (I couldn't request anything while our hosts were moving). You should see them appear very soon.

So this is the little summary for the news lost. Sorry about the forums that will be a week or so late in posts, and that were inaccessible for the past days due to lost configurations on the server-side, but this was to be expected. They are now ready to be used again. I hope this hasn't caused too much inconveniences.

Have fun!
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-07-23T22:33:00Z |title=Virus warning |author=KramerBoy |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Apparently there's a new worm loose on the internet: [ZDNet](https://web.archive.org/web/20021020195547/http://www.zdnet.com/zdnn/stories/news/0,4586,2792260,00.html?chkpt=zdnnp1tp02).

I generally don't report news like this, however today when I opened up my mail box I had three copies of it sitting there, so at least three CamSys fans are already infected.  It seems to me like this whole worm problem could be averted if people could just remember or write down their important email addresses.  Damn technology =\.

**Update:** If you happen to be one of the unfortunate souls bombarding my email with the virus, a fix for it is out: [Symantec](https://web.archive.org/web/20021020195547/http://www.sarc.com/avcenter/venc/data/w32.sircam.worm@mm.removal.tool.html).
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-07-10T22:03:00Z |title=Last but not Least... |author=KramerBoy |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

At long last I get to make my triumphant return to Camelot Systems!! ... Well, not really.  But after a month-long series of delays, GD was finally able to get my accounts activated, and some updates are well overdue.

Most importantly:  StarDraft and StarGraft.  Updates that will promise 1.08 compatibility for both of those programs are currently in the works.  In fact, StarDraft was surprisingly easy to update and is (nearly) complete.  Conversly, StarGraft is proving to be quite the pain in the ass to update and is still rejecting 1.08 patches.  I will continue to work with it, but make no guarantees as to when (or if) I will finish.

That is all, you may continue playing D2 Expansion now.

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-07-08T22:00:00Z |title=A few more changes coming along... |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

As some of you may have noticed, some changes have been made to the Camsys website.

First, the long-awaited Specs Live Wire is now up and running, available at the right menu. It contains the specs of some important StarCraft and WarCraft 2 files for all your editing needs, might you want to start your own editor. These were the latest specs available before the shutdown, but there was more in the archives... that I happen to have ;) I will try finding some time to make the HTML conversion and put them here.

Second, least important but still there, the News Archives section is up. While it only contains the posts of the second Camsys generation, it might be still useful for those of you who have missed important updates or informations.

Among the planned changes, there will be soon a feedback feature implemented in the news post, just like before, so you can give your comments on the news made. On other changes, {{highlight |text=Otak}} is planning to be adding a small new section to the site soon, as well as creating the software pages so you can know the exact use of all the displayed softwares in the Live Wire. Speak of which, more old programs are to be soon uploaded on it.

There is also upcoming projects in progress that are going to be displayed at Camsys, which should keep the activity going, but I will let the authors themselves talk about that when they'll want :)

And, last but not least, the Starcraft {{highlight |text=AI Tournament}} information and signups are going to be available soon! So, a notice for all Starcraft AI scripters, stay tuned and prepare well the script that will be fighting for you in the arena ;)

That is all for now. More updates will be coming!
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-22T19:03:00Z |title=They started without me! *grumbles* |author=Langweilig |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Well, here I've been waiting for CamSys to get back up for what?  Six months?  And the one time I'm gone the news posting ability ACTUALLY comes up?  I feel so betrayed =P  Anyways, right now I'm posting from Carnegie Mellon (college touring) and thought that I should say "Hello" to the CamSys community.  Hello. {{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-22T06:16:00Z |title=A New Direction |author=Defenderz |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Camelot Systems started on WarCraft, then grew and thrived on Starcraft; however, with StarCraft fans slowly leaving, CamSys's community, while still much alive, is only a shadow of what it was a year ago.  This problem was compounded with the fact that {{highlight |text=King Arthur}} left, and the website gained MAJOR hosting difficulties (leaving it offline for an elongated period of time).  Blizzard has recognized this loss in users of StarCraft, and they have taken action with a new patch.  While partially successful, even Replays (and Editors) can't bring us back to our previous peak, when StarCraft flourished.  While the Replay Editor will be helpful for us, the answer in the long run is not in a new editor for StarCraft; we recognize that it alone will not push us up to a new peak.  We must venture to other games in order to gain the plenary support we once had.  Many of us have been wondering and debating what game(s) would be best to create customization for.  I feel there is no better a way to find an answer to this question, then to ask the community, itself, what games it would like to have customization for.  I would like to urge all of you who are reading this to [e-mail](mailto:Defenderz@camsys.org) me with suggestions for a game or games that you would like to see customized.  Be as specific as you want in how it should be edited, or as generic as you wish.  Any other suggestions will also be well noted.  Thank you in advance for your e-mails which I hope will assist Camelot Systems in being what it once was, because hey, "Legends Never Die."
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-22T00:06:00Z |title=Update on Replay Editor |author=Rangsk |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I'd like to start off by saying that people continue to email me saying that I should not create this replay editor because it would make all replay files illegitimate.  I really wish people would read the complete post before emailing me.  I will create a watermark/watermarks to ensure that people know that the replay files are edited.  These will be very difficult to remove.  Now that that's cleared up...

Blizzard has given me the green light for this project, as long as it cannot be used for cheating.  Therefore, I cannot post the replay file format since someone could then create his (or her) own editor without a watermark.  However, I believe that my editor will suit the needs of anyone who has a use for editing replay files, so no one will have a need for the spec sheets (and if it doesn't, feel free to drop me a line after I release the program and I will attempt to add the feature).

As for those who want an ETA for the replay editor, all I can say is "when it's done."  I have been making progress, but it is slow work.  It could be a week (not likely) or it could be 3 months.  Only time will tell.  Finally, I know many of you are extremely eager to help me.  I'm grateful for the overwhelming support, but sometimes too much help isn't a good thing.  Already people are stepping on each other's toes, so I regretfully must decline any further requests to help.  However, I don't want to discourage you from attempting your own editor, as long as you ensure it cannot be used for cheating.  I'll keep you updated as I make progress.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-21T15:46:00Z |title=Question for the public... |author=Otak |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Fo those that still play StarCraft, what types of games do you still play? (IE: Melee, Use Map Settings, FFA, etc...) [Email me](mailto:Otak@camsys.org?subject=StarCraft Types) what types you still play, if you quite frequently play all of them say that... Anyway, My main purpose of this post is to take a survey is to ask if you, the Camelot Community, would actually play a patch that I am still, and have been working on for the past week or so. If you guys aren't interested, that's alright, I'll still finish the thing, but not put as much effort into it... So email me your oppinion. Oh! Heh, the patch specs:

**Current Progress:**
- I have about 5 modified guys, using Arsenal II and [ICE](https://web.archive.org/web/20021020195536/http://cgi.camsys.org/download.cgi?id=ice)
- Chars added so far are: Me ^^, {{highlight |text=Gold Dragon}}, {{highlight |text=Merlin}}, {{highlight |text=Krammer Boy}}, and {{highlight |text=King Arthur}}
- I plan on adding the rest of the Camelot Staff
- Also, I hope to make it into some sort of RPG/UMS

Now, don't expect something spectacular, cause it's been a while since I've messed with StarCraft, but dont expect it to be the worst thing you've ever seen...

Once again, email me your oppinions,
-*Otak*
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-20T06:05:00Z |title=Another CamSys Project |author=Imaginary Number |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

This project is not really an editor like all of the other programs that come out of CamSys. Here are my thoughts on it. When you are hacking you usually need some music to distract you. Why should you have to download it when you already bought the games (or maybe you have not, but the point is that you have it). So I made an MPQ Player (no, that is not the official name, so don't start naming my programs SCLegacy) so you can listen to the music in MPQs without extracting it first. So that is what it does. I already have it done, I just need a GUI for it. I may be able to get something out before I leave for vacation in a week, but I make no guarantees. Anyway, I need some feedback. What are your thoughts? Also, if you're a good artist I need a cool GUI like the one when StarGraft is first opened. If anyone thinks they can do it, e-mail me.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-18T18:57:00Z |title=Conclusion on Replay Editor |author=Rangsk |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Everyone look at {{highlight |text=Otak}}'s update, yes, there is one down there somewhere :)

From what I can tell, there is a general consensus on the replay files.  The editor could be really fun to use and a great asset to custom creators.  Many people have emailed me saying, "Go for it" and saying that this project is one of the best ideas in a while.  However, there is one concern almost everyone has pointed out.  It would, in a second, totally ruin the whole use of replay files.  One of the main purposes of replay files is to prevent hacking in games.  If a person could simply edit a replay file, then who's to say who did the actual hacking.  So, I have come to this conclusion.  As a member of Camelot Systems and as a member of the Starcraft Community, I cannot and will not release a program that could allow a person to lie or cheat using replay files.

I will try my very best to add some sort of mark or marks to the replay file that make sure people know that it was edited.  Also, this means that I cannot release information about how these replay files work (for obvious reasons).  Mirroring what KA did with StarGraft, I will attempt to get in contact with Blizzard and get their feelings on the matter.  If they say not to do it, then I, of course, will not do it.  I would like your opinion on this matter.  Should I forget the project now, should I continue and attempt to create a mark of some sort, or should I change the project to something else (please specify).

I would like to bring to your attention the way replay files work.  As far as I know, they simply store unit locations and actions.  Editing them would be consuming work.  It would require putting in every single keystroke each person does at the proper time.  This is fine for creating things such as movies, but for editing games the only real thing you could do would be to add text (I'm hoping to find some sort of way to do this, although it’s not a promise) and change character names/colors (which I may not include, for obvious reasons).  So, please [Email](mailto:Rangsk@camsys.org) me with your opinion.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-18T05:54:00Z |title=Camelot's Tutorial Tower has been built, M'Lord |author=Otak |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Well, first I would like to say sorry to {{highlight |text=Rangsk}} for posting only 12hours after he did, but... What can I say? ^^

Ok, as my little subject says, I have uploaded the [Tutorial section](https://web.archive.org/web/20021020195536/http://camsys.org/tutors/) (Finally ^^) I'm almost positive all of the links work, but for some horrific reason that one doesn't, just tell me. There are a total of about 14 tutorials up - 3 WarCraft tutorials, the rest StarCraft. Hopefully I will be adding more shortly; however, for now, I hope this will help those that have been asking for them... Also, I mentioned on the Forum somewhere (I think in the Round Table) that I've lost track of all the errors, suggestions, and whatnot for the Chatroom Applet, so if someone could [email me](mailto:Otak@Camsys.org?subject=Applet Error) those it would be greatly appreciated...

For those that have tried to email me from before, well, now that will actually *work*. ^^ Along with FTP, I have all of the other new, sparkley features that come with it, so email away! ^^

Anyway,
-*Otak*
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-17T15:43:00Z |title=New Project and Feedback Suggested |author=Rangsk |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

For the past couple weeks, I (and most of the regulars at #Camsys) have been trying to figuring out the format for the new replay files.  I have made some progress, but it has been much more difficult than originally expected.  If all goes well, I will release a program that can create and edit Stracraft and Brood War replay files.  I've thought of many cool uses, which range from demonstrating the perfect build order or creating 'movies' for Starcraft.

I would like your feedback on this program.  If I recieve mostly negative responses about this project, I may just give it up because it is very difficult.  However, if I recieve support, then I will continue until the support runs out.  You can post your comments/suggestions in the Forums (please put a meaningful subject so I know to read it), or you can [email](mailto:Rangsk@camsys.org) me.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-17T05:46:00Z |title=Second part of the restructuration: the staff appears! |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Another short post! Don't worry(or party) though, it's not going to be a trend from me ;)

{{begin-newsitem |time=2001-06-15T22:02:00Z |title=First part of the restructuration done: the castle's forums |author=Veldhar[GD] |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

This will be one of the rare occasions where you'll see a short post from me, so pay attention ;)

Just a small note to tell you the script restructuration of the site is finally going on. In fact, it had started a few days earlier, but a slight problem with the server unfortunately prevented the move a few days ago, so the forums have remained closed in the meanwhile. Sorry if it has caused any inconveniences.

Please take note of the new URL of the forums: [http://cgi.camsys.org/cbbs/UltraBoard.pl](https://web.archive.org/web/20021020195536/http://cgi.camsys.org/cbbs/UltraBoard.pl)
