{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-06-10T01:48:18Z |title=Success and problems... |author=Gold Dragon |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I have good and "bad" news for ScAIEdit III...

Well, first, the good news :) I have made some "real" tests with the AI compiler, in other words, I've tested the compiled aiscript.bin file generated by ScAIEdit III in StarCraft. After few SC crashes, in which I had to restart my whole comp(!), and thus many bug fixes, it finally works. I've started a Free For All game involving the three biggest AI scripts, the Custom ones, it hasn't crashed and comps were doing their business. So this is very good news, indeed :) After telling this to some friends over ICQ, the same question has almost immediately been asked : "Is it near beta now?". I'll answer this once and for all : No, it's not as near as you think :) There is still many things to do, like BW scripts support.

Speaking of that, I'd like to clarify something. Many think that BW scripts support means to be able to make BW units or use BW opcodes. **This is not true!** What it really means, is the ability to save scripts in the bwscript.bin file. You might think it's not a big deal, but you have to consider the two files, aiscript.bin and bwscript.bin are making references to each other, and the structure of the bwscript.bin file is slightly different. Plus, the limit size of an AI Script File is of 65535 bytes. The original aiscript.bin file has about 62050 bytes(1.05), so it really doesn't leave much place for new scripts. While you can still delete all the "useless" mission scripts(like it will be done in the default template AI script file), you can use bwscript.bin to get another 65535 bytes, and the original bwscript.bin only takes about 26458(1.05) bytes. Only needed to clarify this.

Other than that, I still have to implement references to ASC files in the AI Script Compiler, so you can actually modify the AI Script Files(pretty essential, eh? :) This is only an example of what's left, so the beta is not as near as you'd like, sorry. But still... :)

Now I guess you're waiting anxiously for the bad news ;) Well, I started making BW scripts support tonight, and I just remembered a frightening thing : the original bwscript.bin file is a real **nightmare**! There is many portions of code unused, or links to other parts of scripts, and A LOT of unknown opcodes, to give you an idea of the insanity. And what had to happen happened, ScAIEdit III crashed when compiling the BW scripts because of the obscure composition of that file. It seems that the easiest solution would be to recompile the whole file, to delete all the useless stuff, replace the outside links by the referred blocks, in brief, make a clean file out of a mess ;) Ouch!

This lead me to the following : What should I do about this? I can either ignore the current BW scripts made by Blizzard, which are only mission scripts but contains some interesting things, and continue the normal development of ScAIEdit III to put time on the BW Blizz scripts later, **or** primarily spend my time on solving the problem and make sure all Blizz BW mission scripts can be read and are clean. You want a more simple question? Here it goes : *What priority should I give to the task of solving the BW scripts made by Blizzard?*(this only extend to the Brood War mission scripts) Should I do it first or later? I really need your opinion, because it will have heavy influence on the goal of my work during the next days. This will still be done, but *when*, I want to hear the answer from you. Until I get some input, I'll work on some other features of ScAIEdit III, before taking the decision about this. Thanks in advance!

That's all for my update. Tell me if I should make bwscript.bin research now or later(above), and I'll be glad :) And know that ScAIEdit III is coming!!
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-06-07T02:48:29Z |title=Just like Frankenstein experiments... |author=Gold Dragon |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

ScAIEdit III progress update :

I was really happy tonight when I finished coding the AI Compiler. I clicked on the button to compile the scripts, and waited... waited... Until that "AI Compilation done" box appeared. Puzzled, I restarted the operation, but this time with my watch checking the time... 27 seconds! It took an awful 27 seconds only to check all the scripts and compile the aiscript.bin file. Ouch! So that's what they call "optimization", eh? :)

Staying calm(I tried hard ;), I started by moving the frightening AI Compiler module from the thread to the program itself(in other words, supress multi-threading when compiling). The compilation then took 21 seconds. 6 seconds were wasted only because the Compiler was on a seperate thread, even with priority maxed out. Damn Windows :) But 21 seconds was still unsatisfying, no need to explain why... So I isolated the modules, calculated the time it took, until I found out it took 6 seconds to convert them to ASC files and check for all scripts. Not bad. And 15 seconds for compilation. Ah-HA! Many other isolations and tests later, it appeared 10 seconds were eaten by the module to extract block names and offsets. Ack! After other tests, I saw Windows doesn't seem to appreciate that I resize my dynamic array on every pass so it wasted a lot of time there, and after doing a bit of rewriting, I finally saved up about 10 seconds, reducing the compiling time to 11 seconds. I squeezed another second by reorganizing a little part of the script compiler, for a total of 10 seconds!

Phew :) Even though it now take 270% less time to compile, I'm not totally satisfied : the BW scripts aren't even included! Sure, there is about 362 kilobytes of code on 137 files-scripts compiled, so 10 seconds is maybe not so much, but it's not really good to make many tests. Squeezing another few seconds out of this time would really satisfy myself. But anyways, this is not too bad because when using the Pre-Compiled AI files, the compilation time is almost imperceptible :) So to have a long compilation time, you'd have to make quite big scripts, and a good amount of them. And even then, ScAIEdit would generate other Pre-Compiled AI files for your scripts, so if it ever takes time, it won't happen often :)

All of this to say I've finished and started optimizing the AI Compiler. I wanted to test it tonight, but as you can see, the optimization took most of my time away. It was worth the time though :) Without counting the lots of bugs I've squashed in general(most of them were really little, but pretty annoying :) I should do the first test of compilation on StarCraft tomorrow. The next steps before the Bronze beta test are the support for BW scripts, customized AI scripts(make references to ASC files instead of the AI file itself), CWAD support(shouldn't be long, only direct the saved files to the CWAD module) and some little extra necessary details. That will be a giant step towards public and/or final release :)

Sorry, I don't have any screenshots for this update. It's understandable since I'm working on code and not on design ;) And as always, thanks to everyone supporting me on the (hard) development of ScAIEdit III :)

Until next update...
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-06-06T02:52:16Z |title=Poll results, new one coming... |author=Gold Dragon |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

**ScAIEdit series:** 30(8%)

**ICE:** 13(3%)

**TileEdit:** 3(0%)

Total votes : 350

It's not a surprise to see StarDraft getting 50% of the votes :) StarGraft and Arsenal are almost tied, ScAIEdit comes after, followed by ICE and finally TileEdit. There was a fierce fight between Arsenal and ScAIEdit in the beginning, advantage often switching by only a few votes, until the end of the poll, where Arsenal got a big boost of votes propulsing it to the same level as StarGraft. Thanks for voting! :)

The new question can be seen at the same location as before(on the right menu bar), and the credit for this question goes to Daduke. This poll will end next week too.
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-06-02T02:35:54Z |title=It's getting closer... |author=Gold Dragon |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Here's my usual ScAIEdit III update :)

What I've been up to these days? Well I fixed the vote script, as you must have noticed, but I doubt you want to hear about it, right? :) Okay, okay, I haven't worked as much on ScAIEdit III as I would have liked to, mainly because school is demanding more and more of my free time. If you want to know, after this week, only one normal week of classes is seperating me from finals, so if you're or have been a student, you know the big rush I'm into :) That's not all, there's still a good amount of work waiting for me next week, without counting the finals preparation I'll have to do... So if you notice I'm a bit less active publically these times, don't be surprised, it's most likely to last a week or two before things get back to normal(summer vacations! :).

On ScAIEdit III itself, I've still done a good amount of work on it. No, the AI compiler is still not completed, before you ask. I mainly corrected the last bugs that appeared in the ASC3->AI converter while starting the compiler module. Now everything seems to be okay(the compiler module now reports no error in all of the scripts, looks good), so I'll be able to start the "real" thing tomorrow, generating the first AI Script File with ScAIEdit III. That's exciting :) Oh, speaking of that, if anyone knows where I could find the 1.05 EXE patch for StarCraft, I'd be thankful. Don't ask why I need it, please :)

Now on the planning side, I've got more interesting ideas coming. On my all scripts Syntax Check performed before the AI Compiler can start doing the job safely, I noticed it took a noticeable time before the task is done(5 seconds). Since it's not really good for fast job, and considering this doesn't even count the real compilation, I've came with the idea of "Pre-Compiled AI Files". What does that weird term means? Well instead to make ScAIEdit III compile the same scripts over and over, and thus wasting an awful lot of time, ScAIEdit III will directly use the pre-compiled AI files, saving a considerable time. Of course, it will be only done with Blizzard scripts, since they are everywhere, although you could put your own pre-compiled scripts, even if I don't see the interest of doing this :) Is it just me or I'm orienting myself on a sort of real scripting/programming language? ;)

So far I've received very good input and appreciation on ScAIEdit III, some even pointed me it looked professional(mainly the Syntax Checker part :). Thanks a lot for your comments, it really brings my motivation up to give the best of myself on this. I'm really satisfied of the scripting engine I've done so far, so satisfied I'm probably going to use it again for all my future projects requiring a script language :) The main thing is that I've focused on writing reuseable code, which has a really good effiency over other methods.  I guess there's no need anymore to prove it, except experimenting it yourself. Forget what I just said if you're not a programmer ;)

Oh, a little note : please stop asking me about the scripting engine I've done, how I did it and if I could explain the way it works. That's not because I want to keep all of it to myself, or don't care about your demands, but that would take a good amount of time to explain, and since I receive many requests for it, that makes the job even harder. Maybe later, but not now, please :)

[Intentional mistakes to see the pre-compiler phase](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/http://www.camsys.org/golddragon/scai3/scai3_shot6.jpg)

That's all for today. Gee, there's a lot of text on that box. Just tell me if my long posts bother you, if it does, I'll try shortening the next ones. If it doesn't, well... say it anyway :)

Until next update...
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-05-26T02:59:09Z |title=It's beginning to be fun! |author=Gold Dragon |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

There is a simple reason for this post's topic : I've finished the Script Syntax Checker tonight for ScAIEdit III :) And best of all, it works really fine. You can take a look on the link below :

[Some mistakes intentionally done to see how ScAIEdit III reports them](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/http://www.camsys.org/golddragon/scai3/scai3_shot5.jpg)

Now no one will be able to make mistakes now ;) With this important feature done, the next step is exciting : the AI file compilation. What it means in simple words, is a feature to be able to generate an AI Script file readable by StarCraft so we can see our own scripts in action. That will also be a crucial step, because this feature, no matter what, has to work perfectly without bugs, otherwise the whole program is useless... I saw the disastrous effect it had on ScAIEdit II, and this will be the point I'll focus the more on. Once this big step is done, now I'll have things under control, and maybe be able to announce a possible release date :)

Another good news : I've received the CWAD functions necessary to implement CWAD support on ScAIEdit III today, thanks goes to KramerBoy :) I should start this one right after the main important features of ScAIEdit III are done, like AI compilation. So for everyone who really wanted to have it, you can be happy now :)

Feel free to give your comments/suggestions as usual, I'll really appreciate :)

Until the next update...
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-05-23T02:21:30Z |title=Week-end report |author=Gold Dragon |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I must say this 4-days weekend I had was fairly productive. Well, first of all, if you haven't noticed the 100+ responses in KB's post below, most of them have been written in 4 days, so it's not only me who has been productive ;) If you feel scared in front of this armada of texts(I understand you), just know that KB paid his punishment for proclaming itself Czar, so the peace is finally restored at Camelot. (This was only a friendly war, don't worry :)

As for "serious" stuff, I've been working on ScAIEdit III too. Although I've been busy with Real Life(tm) for 2 days in that long week-end, I still had some time to work on it for the 2 days remaining :) So, I guess you want to know what I've accomplished so far...

First, I've got the ASC3 scheme completed, now I have a clear idea of how they will be designed. There will be many similarities with the previous versions, but the command assignment will be more close to the real thing, opcodes and parameters. For example, instead of writing 'marine = 2' as you could do in the 2 first versions of ASC files, ASC3 replaces this with 'train 2 marine'. Not a big deal you might think, but this brings more power to ASC files, as all the opcodes will be supported that way. Another new interesting feature will be macros. You'll be able to put some ASC code in a text file, and recall it using the macro name you want. Cool, eh? :) A nice example is for those who already know AI scripting : You know these 'Set General Command Target' you have to put after built units? Well using a macro, you could put both of these call only writing this '!build 1 scv' and the macro will put the 'build' and 'set_gencmd' call automatically on the compilation. The '!' tells ScAIEdit III that you're calling a macro, in this example named 'build'(no link with the actual real command). In fact, I'll include it on the release. **Don't worry** if you don't understand anything I've just said, all will be so clear once you'll have it in your eyes :) Why do I feel I'm like explaining a crazy scientific theory no one understands? ;) Perhaps it's because my explanation is a bit dark. Anyways...

After designing ASC3 files, I've written the **AI->ASC3** converter, and it's actually working really good :) Maybe some tweaks will be needed, but the generated code is actually near perfection and working. Now **ASC3->AI** will be more difficult, as the program will have to check the ASC3 syntax, report mistakes, and all. But I've already started on this, and with some luck I should get through it quickly. Once this is done, 'Script Compiling' will be the next important feature to add. After that, it will be test time to see if StarCraft likes the generated AI file :) After this step, things will be lots more interesting, as I'll work on extra stuff, leading eventually to the beta and public release. No, I'm not pointing any release date as long as I don't feel I have things under control, sorry.

To give you an idea of the newest window called 'AI Script Code Editor', I have posted a new screenshot on the link below. It shows the ASC3 file of the AI Script 'Terran 11 - Zerg Town' generated by ScAIEdit III. Notice the seperations the generator made, so the code is easier to read. I had fun writing ScAIEdit III code, as you can see :)

[First glimpse to a real ASC3 file generated by ScAIEdit III](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/http://www.camsys.org/golddragon/scai3/scai3_shot4.jpg)

That's all for today. Feel free to put your comments/suggestions as usual :)
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-05-14T03:16:04Z |title=Camsys.Post.Add(ScAI_Update); |author=Gold Dragon |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

As promised, I'm posting more updates on my progress.

So, what I've been doing? Well, I must admit I've spent almost all of my available time on ScAIEdit :) I've been thinking clearly of the interface, and now have a pretty good idea of what it will look like. So I started ScAIEdit III from "scratch" (the interface, I mean). I've almost finished programming the first window of it, named "HDD Navigator"(you'll understand). I've posted a screenshot of what it looks like, and you'll get a pretty good preview of how the interface will be.

[ScAIEdit III in development](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/http://www.camsys.org/golddragon/scai3/scai3_shot1.jpg)

Yes, it's true. The interface is MDI-driven, and in my opinion, will add more flexibility too. If you don't understand what the "HDD Navigator" window does, it simply allow you to navigate through your disk, and when you double-click on an AI file, it will open a new window showing the available AI scripts(you can follow the idea from this...). Still don't understand? You'll see what I mean once you have the program opened in your screen :)

So, in brief, I've did the new interface and done the "HDD Navigator" window. I'll post other updates when I have new features done. Maybe you think the HDD navigator is a match to do and I'm wasting my time : Don't talk too fast :) It's not so easy. Anyways, I have gathered your suggestions you've sent me so far, and I'll try orienting myself through these. Tomorrow I'll do the AI navigator so I should get near the real stuff, AI scripts! It will be only a matter of time until I get to something useful(and not only a simplified Windows Explorer window :).

On a side note, I apologize if you have sent me some e-mails in the past weeks and never got any reply for them. I didn't had much time these last weeks to reply e-mails, but I did read all of them. I'd like it if you could resend me your e-mails so I can answer them, instead of digging up the archives. One exception though, if you e-mailed for tech support, please direct your questions to the [Camelot's Bulletin Board](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/http://www.camsys.org/cgi/cbbs/UltraBoard.pl), which is there especially for these. For all other subjects, please resend your questions at [dkknight@camsys.org](mailto:dkknight@camsys.org) until my new e-mail adress is up. Sorry for the inconvenience.

Until a new update... :)
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-05-13T23:56:23Z |title=Hasta la bye-bye |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Well I'm singing my swan song one day earlier than expected. I had planned on shutting everything down last night and saying my goodbyes to everyone but as fate would have it, that wish didn't come true. It's been raining so much this past week and today especially that I felt my computer was in danger of being exposed to a flood in our basement, so I quick disconnected it and ran with it upstairs. The upshot of all that was I had a sudden violent disconnection from my computer and all my plans went out the window. Or more appropriately down the river that now exists in what once was our backyard. In any case, I would have been packing most of the day tomorrow, so I probably wouldn't have seen most of the people on my ICQ list (translation: the rest of the camsys staff) to wish them goodbye in person. Monday I'll be flying out to beautiful Irvine, CA to work for Blizzard Entertainment until August 11th, essentially a repeat of last summer + 1 month. As I said before, Merlin isn't leaving till about a month from now, although that is likely to make very little difference to any of you :P In any case, in my absence I've left Camsys in the capable hands of KramerBoy, Golden Dragon (gonna take a while to get used to that change DK... err GD), Nossirahdrof, Ius and DI. My apologies for being pretty silent to you all in the last month or so. Diablo II testing and the end of the semester proved to be a bad combination for keeping in touch. In any case, please don't bother to email me over the summer. In the unlikely event I check the email, I certainly won't have time or be in the mood to answer tech support questions.

Note to KB: put up the same notice on the contact page as last year. thanks.

Take care everyone, and I hope you all have as wonderful a summer as I expect/hope to have.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-05-11T03:09:44Z |title=The shade reveals a dragon... |author=Gold Dragon |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I already hear your first question : Who am I? A new Camelot Systems Staff? Hmm, this is not the case. So it is an identity change. Now who would it be? King Arthur? Merlin? KramerBoy? If you answered yes, you're wrong. I'll only say I am the Dark Knight that disappeared some time ago and I'm back with my true form... Need to say more? ;)

*[useless comment]* Now for those purists who say a Dark Knight can't metamorphose in a Gold Dragon(yeah, someone pointed me that, crazy eh? ;) just think reverse : Some dragons have the capacity to shapeshift, so the Dark Knight was a shapeshift of the Gold Dragon, since these can study advanced magic. AH! Everything's planified so don't try finding mistakes on this ;) And please waste your time somewhere else now :P j/k *[/useless comment]*

Back to serious stuff... So, where have I been during all that time, might you ask? I'll simply answer I was going back to my true form :) Very good pretext to save all these details about what have happened in RL(tm) which you don't want to know anyway ;) Now for ScAIEdit... Since my spare time has been rare these last weeks, yes, I worked on it, but not a lot. Some Delphi weirdness slowed down my work, but even more curious, I only get those pretty strange Delphi bugs with ScAIEdit, and I never encounter them on other projects. Maybe I should download that huge patch available on the net, but a 56k modem can hardly do the job quickly :)

Still concerning ScAIEdit, I have got a new design idea which would improve the development speed(ahh), give you more control on your script(ahh!) but could lose some user-friendly features(oh). I'll explain :

Those who follow the project since the beginning(thanks again for your support and patience ;) will certainly remember ScAIEdit I. Very unuser-friendly, but working entirely on text files managed by the program, giving a complete control on what's going on. Many liked, others didn't. With ScAIEdit II, I did my best to increase the GUI interface so new users would know what to do, but it crippled the ease of control. The effect was reverse, those who didn't liked 1 appreciated 2, and vice versa. But even though I tried to make a good interface, indeed some were still lost on what to do, and much more than I had expected... So, asking myself the question, I thought it would be better if I come back with a text control on the script like version 1 did. I'll try to minimize the loss of ease to use though. I'll keep the GUI I developed for ScAIEdit III, but only changing my routines and interface pointing to the ListBox(trigger interface) to a Memo(text interface). I'll also add many extra features like those found in ScAIEdit I.

That is basically what I will do. Feel free to add any input on the idea. If I see everyone's mad at the idea and absolutely want the original-planned ScAIEdit III interface, I'll probably reconsider my decision :) But I really think that way you'll have more control on the development of your script, it will be faster to write your AI and it will look like more a REAL script instead of some trigger-driven interface(which many seem to dislike, now I'm with them too :). In addition, it will be faster for me to write the program, since I won't have to write many extra features like copy/paste while text functions already do that. Just think you'll see ScAIEdit III out earlier than if it was trigger-driven(and so, less buggy too) :) The need for tutorials will be dramatically increased with the change, so I'll need all the cooperation of AI script experts!

I'll start converting tomorrow. For those fearing of an eventual lack of news from me, don't worry: I'm on a new form, remember? :) I won't do the same mistakes as before, you can be sure. I'll also take in note everything I have to do so I won't forget stuff anymore. In this spirit, I'll also take care of the board, updating the Help system and prepare the long-awaited moderators. I won't do that immediately though, but later this week(or week-end). I'll also have to update the Dark Knight references pretty soon. Speaking of that, don't try my e-mail link above on the post, the mailbox is not up yet. I'll notify when it is working.

Phew! Pretty long post. Don't forget to give me feedback on the new ScAIEdit III idea, and thanks for reading all this text :)
{{end-newsitem}}

> Dear Diablo II beta testers,
>
> As we promised, we wanted to notify you that the end of the closed beta is nearing.  So, if you want to level your character up just a little more or take on Andariel one more time, you should log on sooner than later and enjoy a few last hours of playing before the first phase of beta testing ends.  We anticipate shutting down the closed beta on Wednesday, May 10 at 9:00 p.m. PT in order to prepare for the upcoming Battle.net stress test.
>
> The 1,000 randomly selected closed beta testers will automatically be included in the Battle.net stress test.  You will be sent a new access code and Gamespot URL to download the beta next week.  Please note that the beta is 100 MB and is a subset of what you have been playing in the closed beta.  Only the Barbarian will be playable and there will be three Act I quests.
>
> Thank you again for participating in the Diablo II closed beta.  The time you have spent playing along with all your constructive feedback have helped shaped the game.  We hope that the experience has been positive and has made you more excited about playing Diablo II in the very near future.
>
> See you on Battle.net,
>
> Blizzard Entertainment
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-05-04T01:02:12Z |title=OMG is KA dead? |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

If you read the subject, then the answer is "nearly". Most of you probably are assuming that I've been playing the Diablo II Beta nonstop the last two-three weeks. Not true, at least not last week and this week. It's not that the game is aweful (far from it in fact), I just felt that I'd done all I could with what there was to do in the Beta. It's only act 1 after all, and it takes FOREVER to level up after you hit like clvl 18. Since I got the beta 2 weeks after just about everyone else, there's basically no way for me to catch up with the top ladder people. So I basically gave up. Bought a few cool unique items off of gheed, but that's about it. Most of the weapons/armor/etc aren't in the game, so only 2 out of like 10 sets can even be completed. Plus my paladin can't do a lot of damage (at least not as much as most barbarians tend to do), and my skill tree was royally screwed up. I have been playing once in a while, but today since the server was down I finally decided that I just needed to move on. In any case, I'm now done with the spring semester. I have a final tomorrow in compsci math, a 10 page paper due tuesday, and Nova (my computer game) due on thursday. So needless to say, I have my work cut out for me. That's especially true since I switched to Java as my development platform over JavaScript. Java's more robust than JavaScript, and it lets me do a lot of things I desperately needed to do (like read text files for info - something with JS can't do). In any case, I have the java version about... 25% complete, give or take. I like the new version much better than the old JavaScript version, which was mostly not my code. It's also cleaner to read (for the most part) so I think that anyone with a good background in Java and/or OOP would be able to easily read my code and make a similar game with more/different features, etc.

In any case, don't expect more than one to two more updates from me. The first will likely be next thursday, to announce Nova's completion. The last will just be a notice that I'll be gone until August 11th, and that will likely be posted on Sunday the 14th. Which reminds me... I suppose I should explain why I'll be gone until then. Those of you with us last year around this time may recall that Merlin and I got invited to work at Blizzard for the summer. Well they extended that invitation to us both again, and I'm leaving for California on the 15th (yipee!). I will be keeping in touch with KB, Noss, Dragoon, etc while I'm gone so that I can stay up to date on what's going on. My fall semester looks to be fairly busy, so I may not return in the fall in my full capacity. I think if I do anything, it will be developing Nova in my spare time. Outside of that, there aren't any other projects that I'm interested in working on. Diablo II should be out by then (it better be) but there's not really much in the way of customization to be done. Well that's not entirely true, it's just that I don't see why people would want to customize D2... War3 will still be quite a ways away, so that will leave us with just SC still... I'll be taking a compiler course in the fall that's supposedly very challenging (at least in terms of the workload involved), so I will likely be unavailable for most of the fall, probably the spring too. That will be my junior year, and frankly I wouldn't be surprised if the last you hear of King Arthur is sometime next year...

So anyways, I'll be working night and day now until next thursday on my game. After that, it's only a few short days till I'm off for the summer. I should mention that Merlin will still be around for about another month (because he's finishing up his senior year of HS), but that's not likely to matter much because I don't think he's realistically going to get anything done in the next month (at least not based on past trends). Too much robotics for him :)
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-04-19T12:40:12Z |title=Diablo 2 Beta Report |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Well I'm at school now and am unfortunately unable to play any Diablo 2 at the moment, so I thought I'd share with you my thoughts on the Beta, specifically dealing with the different character classes for now since a lot of people have asked about this particular topic.

Since I gave a testimonial for the paladin and the barbarian, and since you wanted to hear about the other three classes, here goes:

**Amazon**: I like a "javazon" the best, i.e. an amazon that uses javelins/spears/etc rather than the bows and such. I guess cause it still feels like I'm in the midst of combat, rather than sniping from far away. Not that sniping is bad (it's great in Unreal Tournament for instance), but there's not as much fun when "sniping" in D2 cause you feel like you're out of the action. She's got a really good skill tree, allowing you to tailor your amazon to be either a really good bow or spear warrior. Or a combination of both, able to switch when the situation calls for it.

**Barbarian**: Hands down this guy has the best weapon capabilities of any character in the game. And this is really appropriate for this type of character, because he's going to be doing almost exclusively melee combat (unless for some reason you want your barbarian to be a wussy bowman). The advantages given by the ability to weild two weapons at once are incredible. Sure you don't have a shield, but with two weapons at hand, and especially with the Double Swing skill, you'll hardly give any monsters a chance to get a good hit. Bash is also a great skill, as it adds quite a bit of damage and accuracy to your attacks, for only a few mana. Originally I thought I wouldn't be putting hardly any stat points into Energy, because I figured well he has no spells. Well, believe me it's worth it. Most of his good skills do use mana, especially the combat related ones (like Bash). The only thing that prevents the barbarian for being the best character in the game (in my opinion that is) is his warcries. In practice they don't seem to be very useful. A few of them act similar to the paladin's auras, but in reality you're better off having the default attack as bash, and the secondary as double swing (or visa versa). Hotkeying a warcry might be ok, but I doubt that you'll find the need to use them, especially when you get past Act 1. I don't think I'd get rid of them, however, because they do seem very fitting for his character, after all. But perhaps not have quite so many of them? :) Another skill that might be useful for him is the ability to "Sharpen" or "Repair" on his own, possibly like the Warrior could do in D1. I say this because it seems logical (warriors did sharpen and repair their own weapons when needed), and running back to Charsi or a later equivalent does get tedious after a while. Other than that the Barbarian is an excellent character for anyone that likes melee fighting. The only thing you have to worry about is healing potions. The paladin doesn't really have that much of a problem (thanks to Prayer), but the Barbarian definately does (unless you can get one weapon that steals life, and another that steals mana - which I have done and has proved very useful).

**Necromancer**: This guy can be fun, but only at higher character levels. I like the fact that he almost always has a big posse around him, but again this is sort of like the amazon, it can begin to feel like it leaves you with nothing to do, especially if you have a paladin healing your minions (i.e. meaning you don't have to spawn more). The so called "Norman Bates" attack is a riot for low level necros, but it gets old real fast. I've picked up a few REALLY good Necro wands (that you can buy at 25000+ and sell at 5000+ - most expensive in the game), and so typically those do much better (if you can find them). Most of the good ones boost the Necro's skills a lot, and with the Necro that's very important because he's not a real physical type of fighter. If you find yourself relying more on physical attacks, or trying to pump up your necro, perhaps you chose the wrong character class. That's not to say you can't play him that way, but he's not really meant for that. Although in a pinch it can be nice to be able to hack and slash almost as well as the paladin or barbarian (even though you'll be limited to what weapons you can use).

**Paladin**: Not your traditional hack and slash warrior. The barbarian actually fills that role much better, in my opinion, since he typically gets better attack and defense ratings, as well as damage. Don't get me wrong though, that's not to say that the Paladin is a pansy. Far from it, in fact. His auras make him in my opinion the best character class in the game, especially in team combat situations. Having a paladin around to heal you as you duke it out with the really high level monsters (Andariel for example) is incredibly useful. One might say necessary, even. The paladin requires a bit more "hands on" control than the Barbarian, however. I'd suggest the barbarian as a starting character for traditional melee characters, and then moving up to the paladin for some cool (and useful) differences. One of the keys to being a successful paladin is properly managing your auras. That is to say, knowing when each aura is useful, and being able to quickly switch between them. Having this ability can literally mean the salvation of either yourself or your party. The barbarian doesn't have to do a lot of skill changing (which is why I think he's easier to play), but the paladin must in order to be effective. One thing that I didn't realize until too late was how effective a skill Sacrifice and Zeal are. In fact most of his Combat skill tree is much more effective than one might think at first. Certainly his auras make him nearly immortal, Prayer in particular. However I've found that his combat skills have saved his life time and again as well, particularly when you get around to facing the high level monsters like the Banished, the Afflicted, and the Arachs. They are especially useful when facing unique monsters and their minions or champions (who are usually in packs of 3 each). Dumping skill points into Prayer early on is a must for any paladin who doesn't want to be constantly knocking back potions. That also means that you need to invest at least one to two skill points per level-up into Energy, in order to provide Prayer with plenty of Mana to restore your health with. Thankfully Prayer is the only aura (I think) that uses Mana, so if you're out of mana, you should switch to an alternative aura (such as Defiance or Might - both of which will help to clear out a room faster now that your Healing rate is slowing down). This last aspect is important, as it gives your mana a chance to recharge, and helps you to make sure that you're not facing deadly fireballs or those nasty lightning balls the Afflicteds shoot at you for longer than you should be. Plus boosting your Attack rating to some ungodly number that usually only the Barbarian is capable of is quite useful as well :) In short, the Paladin is my favorite character in the game, probably because of his vast capabilities, his usefulness in team play, and how well he fits my personality :)

**Sorceress**: I think I've played her the least of any of the classes. I was going to say aside from the amazon, but I just remembered that I played the amazon quite a bit actually last summer. Not as much as the paladin but enough so that I got to see her look cute in plate mail as she made it to act 3. In any case, the Sorceress, of course, has one of the most fun skill trees of any of the characters. Except for maybe the Necromancer, all the other skill trees seem pretty grim, or at least so concentrated on combat that you lose out on some cool effects, etc. I guess what I'm trying to say is that the Sorceress has some of the coolest spells and effects of any of the other characters. She's able to specialize in Cold, Fire, and Lightning skills (as far as I can remember), which means of course that you can make a jack of all trades type character or a specialized sorceress on par with Merlin et al.

The whole thing comes down to what character class fits your personality best. All of the character classes rock. Initially I was quite depressed that playing as a paladin, I wouldn't be able to raise golems or cast really cool spells, etc. But the specialization actually works better. Instead of everyone being a carbon copy of everyone else (albiet with different stats) ala Diablo 1, you can now have really cool team games where you augment each other's skills and end up doing a much better job of fending off the baddies. If one monster is really giving you a hard time, then I'm sure it will be cake for a character from another class to finish it off for you. And visa versa of course. Even players of the same character class can still find room enough so that they can all be specializing in different areas and still work well together as a team (the amazon and the sorceress are the best examples of this). The rule of thumb should always be to play with a paladin, however. His auras provide all players on a team with noticable and invaluable effects, which often will save you from certain death at the hands of a large pack of high level monsters (as happens when your character gets up around level 15+). So even if you're not as enthusiastic about the paladins as I am, treat the ones you find with respect, and never take their auras for granted. Beleive me, you'll miss them dearly when they're gone.

Anyways, that's my report as of right now. I've got a few really kick-ass screenshots that I got last night in a battle with a friend of mine, as well as a few solo ones. I'll hopefully be posting them later for you to take a peek at.
{{end-newsitem}}

I've released a new program.  This is not the ASM compiler, but I'm hoping that it can serve as a replacement until I can make the compiler. {{begin-newsitem |time=2000-04-08T19:11:35Z |title=Some bad news... |author=Rangsk |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Unfortunately, I have decided to stop work on my action script compiler.  It turned out to be a project that was too big and hard in order for me to get the quality that is expected by Camsys visitors.  I know a lot of you were looking forward to the program, but there are several other reasons for me stopping.

1. It's nothing innovative or new.  It doesn't allow anyone to do anything that they can't already do.

2. ASM is not very hard to learn for the action scripts.  There are some good tutorials out there.

3. I couldn't really think of how this would be of any benefit to anyone.  The only people who could use it would be those who already know assembly, to tell you the truth.  And, the language wouldn't necessarily save any time in the writing of these scripts, since most scripts are less than 40 lines long.

4. The code generated could possibly be buggy, inefficient, and the language might not support everything that's possible with assembly.

So, basically, I don't feel that at this time it's worth the amount of time, energy, and pulled out hair that I would have to spend on this project to finish it.  Please realize that this project isn't totally over.  If there is still a demand for it when I am more able to program it, I will continue it.  Until then, I will try and think of something else to make, which could be anything from a tutorial to an editor of a totally new game.  If you have any suggestions, feel free to post them in the feedback or forum.
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-04-07T22:14:19Z |title=Sigh, RL sucks sometimes. |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

You know, it figures. I finally find a nice girl that I'm not at all nervous about asking her out. She surprisingly says yes, and even batted her eyes at me, making me sure she must be at least interested in me :) So we made plans monday for tonight or tomorrow to see a movie. Between then and today she decides to go to a play with her roommates. Not that bad, cause she still says tomorrow's open, and in actuality it turns out it'd be better. Then I get another email saying that she's decided to take a spontaneous trip to boston after the play tonight, and won't be back till sunday :( Don't get me wrong, I'm not blaming her cause I don't think she's doing any of this on purpose. I just figure that it's not fair that some guys seem to get all the luck. And unless you're 19 or older and dateless, believe me you don't have it worse than I do.

Then there's the wonderful world of the D2 beta. I worked on the game last summer while at blizz, and am supposedly guaranteed a spot in the beta because of it (heck I'm supposed to be in the credits, too). I even get a list of like 5 ppl I can sign up to go on the beta with me. So I send it in, and try not to bug ppl at blizz cause they're all busy and I don't want to be a pest. So it gets to be beta last week and I just assumed that I'd be sent a copy of the beta, along with everyone on my list (that I turned in in october). Turns out that everyone on my list but one guy got their beta, and the other guy didn't get his because of an address change (we think). Except I'm still sitting here with no beta. I wouldn't mind so much except that I promised to play it with a really close friend of mine, and now she's had it for a week and has been forced (gasp!) to play without me :( Now I know, those of you who didn't get the beta (everyone but daduke I guess) will probably just say well hey um we didn't get it either. But I worked on the game...

Hope your lives don't suck as much as mine. Although if they do, feel free to let me know, maybe it'll cheer me up :P
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-04-07T03:30:09Z |title=ArrEdit (secret project) released |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

After DI released Arsenal Zero, I decided that he had developed an excellent way of editing the dat files. What I've done with the help of DI and Rangsk is to come up with a little bit more advanced way of doing what Arsenal Zero did. ArrEdit allows you to Compile/Decompile dat files, based on Format files that you specify. In this way you can get it to add entries/variables to all files. I should note that doing so won't be useful just yet, you'll have to wait until I add support for it into StarGraft, but the potential is there. Another advantage to using ArrEdit is that it is very customizable. Everything is stored in ini files, essentially, from the formats to the text lists to the dat files themselves (in a decompiled state, that is). ArrEdit is a much different way of editing then you had available in Arsenal. The interface has changed quite a bit to allow for much more customizable settings. Essentially you can make the editor do just about anything you want (well within reason). Or at least in the respect that if you need to edit a list of items to choose from, you can do that. You can make a section use lists instead of just a text box to edit things, etc. The possibilities are endless.

One final thing of note is that the Sound entries section of the Units.dat file is now (finally) working, so if you want to start editing sounds for units/heroes/etc you can go ahead and do that with ArrEdit. I should note that ArrEdit is far from complete. This is sort of a trial release to see what everyone thinks of it. If you like it, then I'll keep working on it. If not, then you'll have to stick with Arsenal style editors.

Note: Something is screwed up with the download script, so here's the direct url:

[Download ArrEdit](/web/20000818043445/http://www.camsys.org/software/StarCraft/AEInstall.exe)
{{end-newsitem}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-04-03T02:52:07Z |title=Phew... I hope this won't happen again. |author=Dark Knight |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Many people have wondered if I've died or something bad happened to me lately. Stop worrying(or hoping ;), I'm all okay. I apologize for the outstanding period of inactivity I've accumulated, the longest one in my career :) No, I don't have a tendance to become like Merlin on updates, I simply got caught in a rush of homeworks for 2 weeks, and last week has been full of term exams, for a total of 3 weeks overloaded of work. Useless to say I've got very little time to work on ScAIEdit or even go on the Internet, right? :) I know, I could have posted a note about this, but I didn't thought of doing one. I wished I would have less work to do, but school is school... Something hard to avoid for me ;) So I took this week-end to relax a bit, to get back to "work" tomorrow.

So after my explainations(sp?) for my inactivity, it's time for an update of my status. First, I want to welcome Daduke, DI, Executionner and Rangsk aboard the Camelot Systems Team :) I hope you'll have as much fun as we all have :) Second, I'd want to update status on ScAIEdit, but I forgot most of the things I wanted to say 3 weeks ago. Oh well, once I get back into it tomorrow, I should remember those, meaning I'll do another update in the next few days where you should have more information on the progress.

One thing I still remember though, I will really need people to write tutorials and related texts to get started using ScAIEdit. Once the first beta is out(it's coming, it's coming...), it will be perfect to start writing these. It will be really helpful for me and everyone, so it will take less time to release ScAIEdit, and it will be more user-friendly that way. I'll compile all the texts I receive in a help file, so hopefully everyone will be able to write their own scripts and not wonder what they are supposed to do :) I'm not asking anyone to give me an answer right now, think of it, and if you decide to contribute, send the text to me and you'll get credit for your work, in the section of the help. Thanks in advance!

I'll really try to update more often, but I don't promise anything. But now, with this news updater installed, it should be easier now so expect more news update from me :) (The old way to post news was a bit time-consuming, explaining my lack of them) All right, it's enough for today.

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-04-01T18:18:15Z |title=Andy Bond and Cameron Buschardt Found Dead! |author=KramerBoy |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

The bodies of Andy Bond and Cameron Buschardt were found last night in a dumpster outside "The Admiral" gay bar in upstate New York.  Witnesses say that the two had been at the bar together for nearly five hours before leaving.  Both were intoxicated at the time of their departure.

Authorities are unsure what the two were doing in the dumpster, but investigators believe the two were killed when it was emptied by one of the city's garbage trucks.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-04-01T07:37:10Z |title=So much to do, so little time... |author=Nossirahdrof |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Well, it has been a long time since I've posted anything here, so now that it's become much easier with this awesome news script, I'll post something (LONG) now.

Basically this is an update post as to where TileEdit stands and where things in general stand with myself and my work.
First, let me say that if anyone is still interested (or was there ever anyone interested?) in TileEdit, don't frett, I'm still planning on releasing another version some day. But the only question is when that day will be.

Now, I'm going to get a little sidetracked, because I have to tell you why I don't have all the time in the world to work on it. The biggest reason is that I now have a full time job (Real Life Money Making Job), and it is taking up most of my time. Add to that the fact that the shifts that I get are usually terrible 4PM-12AM or later, most of the time, which leaves very little time to do anything else. Plus the fact that it is telephone tech support for an ISP, and by the time I'm done a day of it, I don't want to do a lot with computers, especially with fixing things (i.e. writing and bug testing even my own programs).
Other than the job, I am also a little bored of TileEdit and am dabbling in other things. First, with the soon to arrive D2 beta, I'm starting to build a Diablo2 Cartoon Web Page, continuing on where I left off in Diablo1 ([My Old Diablo Web Site](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/http://www.geocities.com/TimesSquare/Alley/9744/index.html)). I'm also going to be actually testing the D2 beta shortly (whenever Blizz sends it out) and will probably spend all my spare time with that and the Cartoon Page.

So finally, where does this leave TileEdit? Well, I'm not sure, I definately want to make another release of some kind. The current build that I have actually has a fully working War2 Tileset Editor with two different GUI's (Basic and Advanced), and I am also working on a "Basic" GUI for Starcraft TileEdit which hopefully will make it much easier to edit everything, the problem is that there is still a lot I don't know about certain values in Starcraft's Tilesets so that puts a hold on the great new GUI. I was/am kind of waiting for Merlin to finish up "BlizzDraft" so that you would actually be able to patch War2BNE, because what would be the point of editing the War2 Tilesets unless you could patch them, but "BlizzDraft" is turning out to be bigger and taking longer than everyone expected.

So anyway, let me know what you think of all of this and what happens, happens, I guess. By the way, I have a new found respect for anyone in any kind of telephone tech support.... AHHH!!!
{{end-newsitem}}



{{heading |level=2 |title=March 2000}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-03-31T10:16:04Z |title=Tiny AZ Update |author=DI |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I realized that it would probably be a pain for most people to make the dat formate files for themselves since not everyone knows a lot of scripting (perl is useful :), so I've made 'skeletal' versions of all of them to add to Arsenal 0. Right now, the ones for units.dat, images.dat, sprites.dat, upgrades.dat, and flingy.dat are complete, the others just need an entry list (they all just have 'dummy' entries right now). I leave the rest up to someone who is motivated enough to copy the list out of Arsenal III if need be. :) Or when I have more time, but the ones I've finished already should be enough because I think Arsenal III will handle the rest correctly. Download the entire thing again [here](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/http://www.camsys.org/cgi/download.cgi?id=az) or just download the format files [here](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/ftp://ftp.camsys.org/pub2/CamelotSystems/software/StarCraft/azformats.zip).

BTW, comments are welcome. I know its not all that user friendly right now, but I'm not much of a GUI kind of guy (90% of my time is infront of a UNIX terminal :P). But if people are interested enough it would be pretty simple to make a better UI just from reading in the text files generated (or better yet, someone else could do it :). The main thing I thought this would be useful for is adding entries to a dat file anyway (when/if that becomes possible).
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-03-30T22:48:39Z |title=Under the weather |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-03-30T22:04:14Z |title=Arsenal ZERO! |author=DI |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Heya. Its me aka Magnus aka DI aka... :) Anyway, I've just followed the recent bandwagon of recruits here at CS. But I've also got a little program I've been working on for the last 2 days (hence the title). While the name may change (I didn't ask KA yet :) the premise is what you expect, its a dat file editing program, though it is not like any of the other Arsenals. First of all, it is not intended for users who need something easy or simple to use; instead it is intended for advanced users who really *really* need a stable version of Arsenal to edit your .dats. Basically, the program takes *your* dat specifications and then generates an ini-like text file from a dat that is easy to edit. And then it can recompile it into a dat. Anyway, see the docs I included for more info.

Right now I've only included a format file for units.dat and am relying on others to help me write format files for the other dats (that's yiou! :). Its really just busy work, nothing difficult. Also, I've included source code in the event that someone would like to make a Mac port or something (its command-line right now)... or in the possibly (hopefully unlikely) event that there are bugs and I don't have time to fix them at the moment. Anyway, enjoy.

[Download Arsenal 0 v 1.0](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043445/http://www.camsys.org/cgi/download.cgi?id=az)
{{end-newsitem}}

{{heading |level=2 |title=February 2000}}

{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-29T22:57:00Z |title=Change of plans... |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I'm taking a physics course this semester on Relativity and Cosmology. Fortunately this course has a final project rather than a final :) In any case for this final project I've decided to do a "true-physics" first person edition of the classic game *Asteroids*. This will be my first real game, and I want to spend a lot of time on it because I want it to be as good as possible. As a result, you probably won't see much in the way of custom tools from me except *maybe* over my Spring break (the 11th - 19th of March). This is a big change from my normal day to day activities. I think that game programming appeals to a lot of you, so I will try to document my progress and post updates/screenshots/demos as things develop. I'll probably end up making the entire thing Open-Source because of this, which is fine with me because I have to turn in the source code when the project is done anyways... So I thought I should let you know what I'm up to, and to hopefully keep you interested in checking up on my progress. The code-name for the project right now is Nova... which may change after a while if I come up with something better. The advantage of that name is that it lends itself very well to a sequel... a la SuperNova :P doh I'd better stop before I get flamed. In any case, the webpage for the project will be located [here](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/Nova/), and I will post updates there as needed.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-26T03:41:00Z |title=ScAIEdit update |author=Dark Knight |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Looks like I'm still not used to post regularly on the projects I'm currently working on :) I just hate to appear inactive when in fact I'm far, really far from that ;) So here we go...

These last times, lots of things happened, in Real Life(tm), in my projects, and some others... Speaking of projects, as I write this, I'm currently working on three(!) projects, that you maybe don't know. In addition to work on **ScAIEdit III**, I also work on [Frag Tag](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.xtremebuilder.com/fragtag/), as well as [EarthBound](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.xtremebuilder.com/ebound.htm), both of them being Descent 3 MODs. So as you can see, I've got a pretty good reason to say I've got little time. I shouldn't have taken so much? Maybe... But since Descent 3 MODding is on pretty early stages, if I can help it to get started, I don't see why I wouldn't ;) Fortunately enough, [Frag Tag](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.xtremebuilder.com/fragtag/) is almost finished(99%), and the other one shouldn't take too long, hopefully.

I haven't neglected ScAIEdit III however. The only reason why it appears to be on stand-by once again, it's because, well, I don't give updates often enough :) Another bad thing is that I found out I wasted **WAY** too much time fixing all the problems that were occuring while displaying a background on the ListBox(script data window). So I finally deleted(well, cut/pasted) all the code related to this and flushed it out, so I can work on ScAIEdit III itself, and not lose my time on such futile details(all that time wasted, frustrating) ;) Oh well, everybody can do mistakes sometimes... Many people e-mailed me to know when the official(or first beta) release will be done. I only have one answer for now : "When it will be done"(I hope it's not copyrighted). As I described above, the pressure is already good enough, I don't need to add another one. However, I've got my personal date in mind which will remain secret, and I'm doing my best to get the first (most stable possible) beta release at that date. Don't worry, I can say this date is under... 1 or at worst 2 weeks :) Don't flame me if it's not out for this time though.

I know many of you reading this right now are in need to make some AI scripts, for their TC, or whatever, since I got lots of messages about this. I'll repeat it again, I'm doing my best to get it done, but I don't want to rush and release something even more buggy than the second version. I'm doing the project in a manner so the code stay clean, and readable. Some may wonder why it takes so much more time to get it done than it was on the I and II versions time, well the reason is simple. I now have more projects and other things to care about. I try to make the code clean so I won't have to do a fourth rewrite. The new version has a lot of new things to consider over the previous versions. See what I mean? :) Good. Oh god, got a lot of things written in that little box ;) I hope you haven't fallen asleep, but it compensates for the lack of updates on that :)
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-26T03:00:00Z |title=Forum notification |author=Dark Knight |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Just a little note on the forums. In the last few days, I have received a lot of e-mails from persons who tried the "Lost Password" feature, and never got anything. This is absolutely **normal**. This function wasn't even supposed to be enabled, since no e-mail reference has been given in the system, making it unable to send any e-mails. Sorry for the inconvenience, if you don't remember what your password was, please [e-mail me](mailto:dkknight@quebectel.com) about it so I can change it and give you the new one(even though I'm the admin, I can't see the password of users, only able to replace them). You may notice that it takes some time for your e-mails to get replied, but that's usually because I've got a lot of things to do(especially in these times). You can be sure every e-mail I receive is read(except those publicity spams). I will try to activate the e-mail features on the board... :)
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-25T00:36:00Z |title=Varous updates... |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Even though I was lucky enough to get out of Writing class all this week, it still seemed to be quite busy for me. I've been doing a lot of work this week with Ius on the command-adding technology. Shadow sent in some especially good suggestions for new commands, and I hope to have them completed tonight or tomorrow:

* Restock with energy / resource cost. Available on all vultures rather than just Raynor.
* Energy attacks - require energy to attack. Useful for magical units that are otherwise defenseless, or for an increased effectiveness for an existing unit's attack. You could, for instance, research a really powerful attack at the Academy for Marines, and when the research is complete, all marines would get a new weapon and an energy bar. Maybe something like hypersonic rifles or something (ala the big guns in Aliens) - this last part was sort of my idea :)
* Convert shields/ health to energy. Useful for the DA especially, who's got a crapload of shields and whose survival may depend on having enough energy to cast feedback or mind control
* Convert energy to shields/health. Ditto for above
* Unit summoning - he and I both realize this one will be difficult, I don't know if I will be able to do it. But I thought I should mention it so that you knew the idea was submitted.

Thanks also to the several other people (including one of the Aliens TC guys) for sending me in your ideas! Keep them coming.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-21T05:23:00Z |title=Two new commands available. |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I've uploaded ot the [Customs Database](/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/cgi/upload/display.cgi) two patches which add two different commands. The first is one that Ius created, and allows you to toggle a switch based on the Act Var. I'd imagine this would be quite useful for RPG maps... you could press a button to transport the hero or something, I dunno. The second patch adds a Restock command to Jim Raynor (Vulture), which brings his count of Spider mines back up to 3. I just got it working to read the Act var for the new count, and I'll post that version tomorrow. The current version works fine, it just doesn't read from the Act var is all. Enjoy! While I've got the motivation, feel free to [email me](mailto:kingarthur@camsys.org) any suggestions for new commands that you have.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-20T06:44:00Z |title=Customs  Uploader and Database Complete |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I just finished the Customs Uploader and Database... As I mentioned before, nothing will show up on the database until it gets approved by an authorized CamSys staff member, Campaign Creations staff member, or an MVP (ala the forums). There will be no ratings on the customs, and for now the only sorting is by title. I'll add additional sorts when I'm sure that the venture has been successful and people are interested enough in it. Also by popular demand, I have added a spot for you to supply alternative links for the custom file and/or image. This is going to be required for people who wish to upload customs greater in size than 4mb. Also, in case you missed it, you can supply an Image as well. You should also be able to edit your customs once they've been approved so that for TC's you can make sure that they're always listed up to date. In any case, get busy submitting your customs below. In a few days, expect to see them being approved. Oh before I forget, the only real requirements for approval are that you're not blatantly copying someone else, that the custom actually works, and that there's no objectionable content in the TC (which would get us in trouble with our host).

[Upload Customs](/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/cgi/upload/)

[Customs Database](/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/cgi/upload/display.cgi)
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-19T05:46:00Z |title=Customs Script Update |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I've been working pretty much non-stop the last couple of days to get the Customs Script finished. I have the uploading part complete, and tomorrow I hope to have the database (listing) portion complete as well. When finished, the script will let you upload any of the 4 different patch formats (PAT, CWAD, SECWAD, SEPAT), supply author information (with optional email) and a general title / description for the conversion. I am also going to make the submissions password protected so that the person who uploads the custom can delete/update it as necessary (note that the password won't be needed to download the actual custom). The database should be able to sort by patch type, author name, conversion title, file size, etc. Again, hopefully the database portion will be finished tomorow. When everything is all set to go, keep in mind that the customs won't immediately appear on the database. They will appear once a staff member approves the conversion. This is being done to try to keep content under control. Also, I may put a 2mb file size limit on anything you upload... just because I don't want to have the server fill up really fast.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-17T00:52:00Z |title=ICE Progress |author=KramerBoy |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Well, after a couple of weeks of dead ends, I finally got the decompiler in ICE functioning correctly.  There are still a few loose bugs left to squash in this thousand-line mostrosity, but most of those will be relatively simple to fix.  The next area to conquer is the compiler, and unfortunately, this may also turn out to be an uphill battle.  I'm confident that I have a sufficient grasp of the techniques involved, but only time will tell.

So what's the overall status of the project?  Well, technically speaking, I haven't even started =).  I'm planning on getting BCB4 soon, and I want to develop the project using that.  My current work is only a practice of sorts, to make sure I can pull off everything I need, and will never actually see release.  Still it is really helping me get the hang of this whole scripting thing =).

Progress is good so far, and I'll be sure to keep you guys updated if anything new comes up.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-15T22:38:00Z |title=Website changes... |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

In an attempt to make pages load faster, I have dumped a lot of the tag properties in favor of specifying defaults in Cascading Style Sheets. I recently downloaded Netscape 4 to make sure that the site looked more or less as expected in that browser, and it appears to fit my expectations so far. Let me know, however, if you are experiencing any problems with any of the pages. Please let me know what browser and what pages give you any trouble. Later tonight/this week I also hope to revamp the news style a bit to cut down on the code used. I cut down on it a lot in the last change, and I hope to do so even more. You can see a sample of what I intend here: [sample of new news style](/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/index2.html). Let me know if you think this is worse... My main concern was to cut down on the site load time by having smaller code, but if it's illegible, I'd like to know :)
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-15T22:34:00Z |title=Customs Submission Update |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Since I have gotten an overwhelmingly positive response in favor of the Customs Submission script, I will be going ahead with it. The majority of you suggested that the customs be in some way moderated - I hope to hire an additional CS staff member to do this. For now Ace and one or two other CS guys will be reviewing them. All customs will be reviewed before they are made public for download on the site. I'll try to have as extensive a database as possible, so that you can choose to display customs based on types of things replaced, topics, authors, size, rating, date, etc. I'm starting work on the script today (just got an excellent book on PERL that should really speed things along), and should be finished by the end of the week if all goes well. Thank you to everyone who responded.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-15T01:58:00Z |title=Customs Submission? |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I think I know enough PERL now that I could write a customs submission script, that would display the customs in a tabular format much like the Bookshelf does, so long as there is interest in it. Please [email me](mailto:kingarthur@camsys.org) and let me know what you think of this idea. It seems appropriate that since we're responsible for nearly all customs related software, that we should have a database of customs made with our programs... so please let me know if you're either interested in seeing something like this, or if you'd like to contribute.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-11T05:30:00Z |title=New Section in the works... |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I had a few hours to kill tonight without access to a compiler to work on StarGraft or my other projects, and I decided that it was about time that I begin work on a project that's been on the back burner for a while... A few people had mentioned to me when I was publishing my programming tutorials to concentrate more on the main topic: game editor programming, and less on programming in general. Point taken. I am right now compiling a list of all the programming books that I own, and will soon be rating each of them to help provide you with the information that you need to make the proper choice, or even to introduce you to books that you might otherwise not have seen. My intent is to have the rest of the CamSys staff (and a few CC guys) join in on this project, so hopefully we will have a wide array of book reviews for you to browse through. At least these won't be the incredibly biased reviews that Amazon.com has... plus they're reviews by people who are active in the community the books are about.

Feel free to peruse my [work in progress](/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/cgi/bookshelf/) and send me any comments/suggestions that you have. I'll be starting the first reviews this weekend (hopefully).
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-11T00:56:00Z |title=StarGraft News |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I've decided that due to the last 17 or so odd buggy releases of StarGraft to stop development of the current version, and start over from scratch. The fundamental problem with the program is that, frankly, it was never meant for public consumption when I designed both the visual and the programming interface... I just basically made the program as quickly as possible, and with as little care as to where I threw code in. I really made no provisions for any easy expansion in the program, and as a result with the more options I add, and the more I try to fix it, the more hidden flaws surface. So starting whenever I have free time, I will be rewriting StarGraft completely from scratch. It probably won't take a long time, assuming that I have the necessary free time to do it in. I think it really needs to be done. So many people have kept complaining of the bugs that keep popping up in the latest "bugfix" that I've just had it, and I hope that this will make things easier on all of us. It'll save me from looking through a lot more code than I should be, and you from the unnecessary frustration of this flawed tool. StarGraft wouldn't be so hard to use, I think, if it actually worked reliably and had an intuitive interface, neither of which it seems to have for 99% of you. So my apologies, and please be patient as I re-develop it.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-08T02:55:00Z |title=StarGraft Updated |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I'm releasing a new version of StarGraft which has several major changes. The first is the change on the button page to a drop-down list for all the buttons a group has. This replaces the trackbar control I was using before. This should make it much easier to use (I hope) and seems to have squashed all of the bugs that were appearing in the button tab. I've also added, thanks to Ius, three new tabs to the program. The first two allow new action / requirement types to be used in the button page. These will all be primarily written by either Ius or myself. You'll just have to paste the appropriate code in and go from there. Eventually this interface will hopefully be improved so that you can make your own req/act types based on templates that we have already created. The third tab is a support one for these two new features. Let's pray that this one will be bug free...

[Download StarGraft v2.2.8; Build 270](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/cgi/download.cgi?id=sg) [781kb]
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-06T22:33:00Z |title=CamSys joins the OrgNetwork |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

I'm pleased to announce that [Camelot Systems](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/) has officially joined the Org Network, a group of high quality fan sites including [StarCraft.org](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.starcraft.org/) and [WarCraft.org](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.warcraft.org/). [CamSys](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://www.camsys.org/) has had very close relations with the Org Network sites since their inception almost three (!!!) years ago, a few months before the release of StarCraft. Without a doubt, the Org Network is responsible for the popularity that CamSys has enjoyed in the last several years. They kept the community appraised of our latest innovations, and provided us with the much needed fan base to support our continued software development. About the only change that you will see is me posting on the Org sites from time to time with major CamSys news (such as software/tutorial releases), and an increased focus on all the games the Org Network sites cover (for now just Blizzard games). We will continue to design software for other games as the staff chooses, but the primary focus will be on Blizzard games.Hopefully this will launch a new era of success and cooperation for our site, and of course the Org Network. Thank you for your continued patronage, and your support/interest in our software.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-06T20:45:00Z |title=Tutorial Progress is slow but sure |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

My apologies that it's taking me a while to get the compiler (and associated tutorial) completed. I'm not exactly surprised that it's not done yet, given that I have much more work to do this semester than I had to do last semester. Plus it is my first compiler, so there's that, too. I'll keep you up to date hopefully a little more often this week on where i stand. I have the assembler (compiler) part mostly completed... just need to add support for a few more tricks and then that should be ready to go. After that I move on to the disassembler (decompiler) portion of it. That hopefully should prove to be easier (since I don't have to parse text or anything, just hex). I'll probably wait to do the tutorials until the whole thing is completed and in good working order.

A couple people asked me about the upcoming 1.08 patch for StarCraft/Brood War, and I thought that I should let you know that I do plan to add support for it in StarGraft just as soon as it gets released and I have a chance to go through and find everything I need. Hopefully it won't take too long. As for the other tools, I can't promise that they'll still continue to work - it all depends on what they change in this new patch. In fact they may even disable StarGraft, but we'll do our best to update the tools to work with the new version. I also thought I should let you know that Merlin is still working on BlizDraft (the new version of StarDraft that will support SC, War2 BNE, and Diablo). He's had to do a couple of rewrites of code, which is why it's taking longer than I had expected it to. He might have something ready by the end of the month, school schedule permitting.
{{end-newsitem}}


{{begin-newsitem |time=2000-02-03T00:17:00Z |title=Star Wars: Open Rebellion News |author=King Arthur |site=Camsys |categories=Camsys}}

Ash1138, the one-man-band leader of the Open Rebellion Total Conversion has just released a very impressive AT-AT test, as well as a pre-release of a slightly older version of ORTC that wasn't previously available to the general public. I highly reccomend visiting the Open Rebellion site to get them and to keep up to date on the latest news from the best TC out there :)

[Open Rebellion](https://web.archive.org/web/20000818043451/http://openrebellion.camsys.org/)
{{end-newsitem}}
